6
Conclusion

We have shown that an interactive search algorithm can emerge from RL training in a transformer agent, even when supervision comes solely from sparse terminal reward. In our analysis, policy gradient updates shape two attention heads into complementary memory-retrieval mechanisms: one suppresses previously tried actions, while the other detects failed outcomes and triggers backtracking. Their composition yields a DFS policy that generalizes from shallow curriculum training to deep trees, and under imbalanced goal distributions, discounting further biases the learned policy toward ranked search. Our work is a first step toward understanding complex agentic behavior in stochastic RL environments, and provides a tractable testbed for future directions including principled memory management, context pruning, and scalable RL algorithms for long-horizon interactive reasoning.

Appendix A
Proof of Convergence and Generalization

A.1
Formal Statements of Theorems
2
and Theorem
3

Below we provide the formal statements of Theorems
2
and Theorem
3
in a merged version, where we use
\mathsf{poly}(k)
to denote a polynomial function of
k
.

Theorem 5

(convergence and generalization results under balanced goal distribution, formal)
.

Suppose (
19
), Assumption
1
hold,
k\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
is larger than some absolute constant,
N=\mathsf{poly}(k)\geq\max\left\{k^{5},\frac{k^{L+1}-1}{k-1}\right\}
,
L
is treated as a constant, and
\eta>0
is smaller than some absolute constant,
\epsilon>0
satisfies
\ln(1/\epsilon)\geq e^{k}
. Let
\delta_{1}=\epsilon^{1.05}
,
\delta_{2}=\frac{\epsilon^{1.02}}{k^{L}}
. Under our training setting, for depth-1 curriculum training, the success rate
R(\pi;{\mathcal{P}}_{1})
reaches
1-\delta_{1}
in
T_{1}=O\left(\frac{1}{\eta\epsilon^{1.1}}\right)
iterations. At the end of depth-1 curriculum training, we have

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(T_{1})};{\mathcal{P}}_{l})=O\left(\frac{1}{k^{l-1}}\right),\quad\forall 2\leq l\leq L.

(34)

For depth-2 curriculum training, the success rate
R(\pi;{\mathcal{P}}_{2})
reaches
1-\delta_{2}
in
T_{2}-T_{1}=O\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\eta\epsilon^{1.1}}\right)
iterations.
After depth-2 curriculum training, given any full
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
with depth
l\in[L]
and any goal node
g({\mathcal{T}})
, we have

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(T_{2})};({\mathcal{T}},g({\mathcal{T}})))\geq 1-\epsilon.

(35)

The proof of Theorem
5
is provided in Appendix
A.2
. Note that in Theorem
5
,
without loss of generality, we assume (the hardest and the most extreme case where)
\ln(1/\epsilon)\geq e^{k}
. If
\epsilon
is larger, the convergence bound in Theorem
5
also holds: the proof still goes through and possibly becomes simpler with a bit of extra discussions, as depth-1 curriculum training may end before
T_{1,i}
for some
i\in[5]
defined in Appendix
A.2
and thus we don’t need to track some sub-phases.

Comparison with related training-dynamics analyses.

As far as we know, ours is the first optimization-level analysis of policy-gradient training in which coupled transformer parameters (
B,C,\overline{P},Q
) coevolve into a two-head memory circuit for agentic search, yielding DFS and depth generalization from depth-1/2 curriculum training to deeper unseen trees. Prior analyses simplify the setting along different axes. Graph-search works typically study static instances whose graph structure is revealed in the prompt
[
12
,
19
,
23
]
. RL planning analyses operate on fixed graph abstractions, where generation amounts to extending a path by next-node tokens and process rewards may expose adjacency or target information
[
20
]
. RL/RLVR reasoning analyses, while using outcome or verifier rewards, largely treat RL as post-training over self-generated reasoning traces rather than interaction with a partially observed environment
[
2
,
6
]
; in these settings the evolving state is essentially the generated prefix, or the analysis assumes/fixes atomic operations needed for reasoning.

By contrast, our transformer is an agent in a hidden stochastic tree: it must infer the search state from trajectory history and sparse terminal reward, while learning to remember failed branches, avoid repeated actions, and backtrack from both failed leaves and exhausted internal nodes. This richer interactive setting also makes the training-dynamics analysis substantially more delicate: the proof must track a multi-phase evolution of interacting attention and output matrices, while resolving the relevant return-to-go terms under both a stochastic policy and posterior uncertainty over the hidden environment.

A.2
Proof of Theorem
5

Notation.
Throughout the proof, we use
o(1)
or e.g.,
O\left(\frac{1}{k}\right)
to represent small quantities that may also be negative. We sometimes abuse the notation to use
[t_{1},t_{2}]
to denote
\{t_{1},t_{1}+1,\ldots,t_{2}\}
for integers
t_{1}\leq t_{2}
.
Though we fix the last row of
P
to be 0 and don’t update it (cf. (
20
)),
its gradient is still useful and we’ll compute it. Note that

\displaystyle\delta M^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{\Phi\sim{\mathcal{P}},\pi_{\theta^{(t)}}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\nabla_{M}\log\pi_{\theta}(a_{h}\mid E^{(h)})\Big|_{M=M^{(t)}}\sum_{i=h}^{H}r_{i+1}\right]\,\,\text{ for }M\in\{B,C,P,Q\}.

(36)

Then according to update rule (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle M^{(t+1)}=M^{(t)}+\eta\delta M^{(t)}\,\,\text{ for }M\in\{B,C,\overline{P},Q\}.

(37)

More generally, for any quantity
\{X^{(t)}\}_{t\in{\mathbb{N}}}
(except for the last row of
P
), we define

\displaystyle\delta X^{(t)}\coloneqq\frac{X^{(t+1)}-X^{(t)}}{\eta}.

(38)

We let

\displaystyle U\coloneqq(u_{0},\cdots,u_{N})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{1}\times(N+1)},\,\,V\coloneqq(v_{0},\cdots,v_{k+1})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{2}\times(k+2)},\,\,Z\coloneqq(z_{0},z_{1},z_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{3}\times 3}

(39)

denote the node, action, and label embedding matrices respectively.
We define the induced matrix
A_{M}

(M\in\{B,C,P,Q\})
as

\displaystyle A_{B}\coloneqq U^{\top}BU\in\mathbb{R}^{(N+1)\times(N+1)},\quad A_{C}\coloneqq Z^{\top}CZ\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times 3},

\displaystyle A_{P}\coloneqq PV\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)\times(k+2)},\quad A_{Q}\coloneqq QZ\in\mathbb{R}^{(k+1)\times 3},

(40)

and let
A_{M}^{(t)}\coloneqq A_{M^{(t)}}
and
\delta A_{M}^{(t)}\coloneqq\delta A_{M^{(t)}}
for
M\in\{B,C,P,Q\}
.
Then by (
37
), we have

\displaystyle A_{M}^{(t+1)}=A_{M}^{(t)}+\eta\delta A_{M}^{(t)}\,\,\text{ for }M\in\{B,C,\overline{P},Q\}.

(41)

We define

\displaystyle\pi_{h}\coloneqq\pi_{\theta}(E^{(h)}),\quad\pi_{h}^{(t)}\coloneqq\pi_{\theta^{(t)}}(E^{(h)}),\quad\widehat{G}_{h}\coloneqq\sum_{i=h}^{H}r_{i+1},

\displaystyle Q_{h}^{(t)}(a)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{\pi^{(t)}}[\widehat{G}_{h}|{\mathcal{F}}_{h},a_{h}=a],\quad G_{h}^{(t)}\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{\pi^{(t)}}[\widehat{G}_{h}|{\mathcal{F}}_{h}]=\sum_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(a)Q_{h}^{(t)}(a),

(42)

where
{\mathcal{F}}_{h}
denotes the
\sigma
-field generated by the history available before choosing
a_{h}
, equivalently by
E^{(h)}
. We also write
Q_{h}^{(t)}\in\mathbb{R}^{k+1}
for the vector whose
a
-th coordinate is
Q_{h}^{(t)}(a)
. Define
N_{h}
as the number of leaf nodes explored up to step
h
:

\displaystyle N_{h,\times}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}.

(43)

At any step
h
, the agent is at node
n_{h}
.
We let

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\{i\in[k]|i\text{ is taken at }n_{h}\},\quad\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
\emptyset,\quad\text{if }c_{h}=\times,\\
\end{cases}

(44)

denote the set of downward actions that are taken at node
n_{h}
. Then for any action
i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}
,
Q_{h}^{(t)}(i)=0
.
We also define

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k+1]:\quad N_{h}(i)\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}\mathbbm{1}\{a_{j}=i\}

(45)

as the number of times action
i
is taken up to step
h
. And we let
N_{h}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq N_{h}(k+1)
.

Gradient computation.

We first derive the expressions of
\delta A_{M}^{(t)}
and the policy
\pi_{h}
in Lemma
1
. The expectations at step
t
are all taken under policy
\pi^{(t)}
. Note that histories used for gradient updates exclude the terminal observation, and thus terminal token
\checkmark
never appears in
E_{c}^{(h)}
.

Lemma 1

(gradients and policy expression)
.

Under balanced goal distribution (
19
), Assumption
1
, we have the following gradient expressions for any
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
:

\displaystyle A_{C}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}a_{c,0}^{(t)}&-a_{c,1}^{(t)}&0\\
-a_{c,0}^{(t)}&a_{c,1}^{(t)}&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\delta A_{C}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}\delta a_{c,0}^{(t)}&-\delta a_{c,1}^{(t)}&0\\
-\delta a_{c,0}^{(t)}&\delta a_{c,1}^{(t)}&0\\
0&0&0\end{pmatrix},

(46)

where

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0\}\frac{N_{h,\times}(h-N_{h,\times})}{\left(N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}\right)^{2}}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(G_{h}^{(t)}-Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)\right],

(47a)

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\frac{N_{h,\times}(h-N_{h,\times})}{\left(N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}\right)^{2}}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})(1-\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}))Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right].

(47b)

\displaystyle A_{B}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}0&a_{b,0}^{(t)}&a_{b,0}^{(t)}&\cdots&a_{b,0}^{(t)}\\
0&a_{b,1}^{(t)}&a_{b,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&a_{b,2}^{(t)}\\
0&a_{b,2}^{(t)}&a_{b,1}^{(t)}&\cdots&a_{b,2}^{(t)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&a_{b,2}^{(t)}&a_{b,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&a_{b,1}^{(t)}\end{pmatrix},\quad\delta A_{B}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}0&\delta a_{b,0}^{(t)}&\delta a_{b,0}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{b,0}^{(t)}\\
0&\delta a_{b,1}^{(t)}&\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}\\
0&\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}&\delta a_{b,1}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}&\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{b,1}^{(t)}\end{pmatrix},

(48)

where

\displaystyle\delta a_{b,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}}{\left(e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left\{\left(|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}\right)\beta_{h,0}^{(t)}-e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}\beta_{h,1}^{(t)}-e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}\beta_{h,2}^{(t)}\right\}\Bigg],

(49a)

\displaystyle\delta a_{b,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}{\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\frac{e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}}{\left(e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left\{\left(e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}\right)\beta_{h,1}^{(t)}-e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\beta_{h,0}^{(t)}-e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\beta_{h,2}^{(t)}\right\}\Bigg],

(49b)

\displaystyle a_{b,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{-a_{b,0}^{(t)}-a_{b,1}^{(t)}}{N-1},\quad\delta a_{b,2}^{(t)}=\frac{-\delta a_{b,0}^{(t)}-\delta a_{b,1}^{(t)}}{N-1},

(49c)

with

\displaystyle\beta_{h,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right),

(50a)

\displaystyle\beta_{h,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)})\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(i)G_{h}^{(t)}-a_{p,2}^{(t)}|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}),

(50b)

\displaystyle\beta_{h,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq-\left((h-1-N_{h}(\texttt{up})-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)a_{p,2}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{k}N_{h}(\texttt{up})a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\quad+(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)})\left(\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}(N_{h}(i)-1)\pi_{h}^{(t)}(i)G_{h}^{(t)}+\sum_{i\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}N_{h}(i)\pi_{h}^{(t)}(i)(G_{h}^{(t)}-Q_{h}^{(t)}(i))\right).

(50c)

\displaystyle A_{P}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}&-a_{p,1}^{(t)}&a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\frac{a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}\\
-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}&a_{p,2}^{(t)}&-a_{p,1}^{(t)}&\cdots&a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\frac{a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}&a_{p,2}^{(t)}&a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&-a_{p,1}^{(t)}&\frac{a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}\\
0&0&0&\cdots&0&0\end{pmatrix},\;\delta A_{P}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}-\frac{\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}&-\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}&\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\frac{\delta a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}\\
-\frac{\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}&\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)}&-\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\frac{\delta a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots&\vdots\\
-\frac{\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}&\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)}&\cdots&-\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}&\frac{\delta a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}\\
\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}&\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}&\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}&\cdots&\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}&-\delta a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}\end{pmatrix},

(51)

where

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}-(k-1)\delta a_{p,2}^{(t)},\quad a_{p,3}^{(t)}\coloneqq\eta\sum_{t^{\prime}=0}^{t-1}\delta a_{p,3}^{(t^{\prime})}=a_{p,1}^{(t)}-(k-1)a_{p,2}^{(t)},

(52a)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\frac{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right],

(52b)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)\bigg(\mathbbm{1}\{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(N_{h}(j)-1)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}G_{h}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\qquad-\mathbbm{1}\{j\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{N_{h}(j)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(j)-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\bigg)\Bigg],

(52c)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\Bigg[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\bigg(\mathbbm{1}\{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(N_{h}(j)-1)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathbbm{1}\{j\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{N_{h}(j)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}\bigg)(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)})\Bigg],

(52d)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\frac{N_{h}(\texttt{up})e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right],

(52e)

\displaystyle A_{Q}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{k}&-\frac{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}}{k}&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\frac{a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{k}&-\frac{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}}{k}&0\\
-a_{q,0}^{(t)}&a_{q,\times}^{(t)}&0\end{pmatrix},\quad\delta A_{Q}^{(t)}=\begin{pmatrix}\frac{\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{k}&-\frac{\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}}{k}&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots\\
\frac{\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{k}&-\frac{\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}}{k}&0\\
-\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}&\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}&0\end{pmatrix},

(53)

where

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\frac{(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})(1-\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}))Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0\}\frac{(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(G_{h}^{(t)}-Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)\right],

(54a)

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})(1-\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}))Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right]

\displaystyle\quad-{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0\}\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(G_{h}^{(t)}-Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)\right].

(54b)

Furthermore, for all
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\pi_{h}^{(t)}
can be expressed as follows:

\displaystyle\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{h}^{(t)}}\exp\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\xi_{h}^{(t)}\right),

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k]:\quad\pi_{h}^{(t)}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{h}^{(t)}}\exp\left(\frac{\varphi_{h}^{(t)}(i)}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}\right),

(55)

where

\displaystyle\varphi_{h}^{(t)}(i)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|a_{p,2}^{(t)}e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}-\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\left((a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)})(e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}-e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(N_{h}(\texttt{up})\frac{a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}-N_{h}(i)a_{p,1}^{(t)}+(h-1-N_{h}(\texttt{up})-N_{h}(i)-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}

(56)

for all
i\in[k]
,

\displaystyle\xi_{h}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times,\end{cases}

(57)

and
Z_{h}^{(t)}
is the normalizing factor:

\displaystyle Z_{h}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\exp\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\xi_{h}^{(t)}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{\varphi_{h}^{(t)}(i)}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}\right).

(58)

The proof of Lemma
1
is postponed to Appendix
A.3.1
.

A.2.1
Depth-1 Curriculum Training

To analyze the convergence of Depth-1 curriculum training, we define the following time points:

\displaystyle T_{1/2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup\left\{t\in{\mathbb{N}}:a_{p,1}^{(t)}\leq 4.1k\ln k\right\}.

(59)

We divide depth-1 curriculum training into two sub-phases by
T_{1/2}
: Phase 1.1:
t\leq T_{1/2}
and Phase 1.2:
T_{1/2}+1\leq t\leq T_{1}
.

Phase 1.1:
t\leq T_{1/2}
.

Define

\displaystyle T_{1,1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup\left\{t\in{\mathbb{N}}:a_{q,\times}^{(t)}\leq\ln k\right\},

(60a)

\displaystyle T_{1,2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup\left\{t\in{\mathbb{N}}:a_{p,1}^{(t)}\leq k\right\},

(60b)

We will show the following dynamic.

•

Before
T_{1,1}
, matrix
A_{P}
is very close to 0, and the agent cannot distinguish if an action is taken or not. During this time period, the agent learns how to go up at leaf nodes that are not goal nodes, and go down at the root.

•

Between
T_{1,1}
and
T_{1,2}
,
a_{p,1}
rapidly increases to
k
, the agent has high confidence to go up at leaf nodes and go down at the root, but is still bad at distinguishing if an action is taken or not when the trajectory gets long.

•

From
T_{1,2}
to
T_{1/2}
,
a_{p,1}
increases to
4k\ln k
, the agent has high confidence to follow all rules at trees of depth 1, and by the end of this period, the agent has a success rate higher than
1-\frac{1}{k}
.

During the whole Phase 1.1, matrix
A_{B}
is very close to 0 and can be neglected.
We rigorously state the training dynamics of Phase 1.1 in the following lemma.

Lemma 2
.

Under balanced goal distribution (
19
), Assumption
1
, for all
t\leq T_{1/2}
, for all
t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T_{1/2}
, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle-\frac{\ln^{2}k}{Nk}\lesssim a_{b,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N},

(61a)

\displaystyle-\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N}\lesssim a_{b,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ln^{2}k}{N},

(61b)

\displaystyle\left|a_{b,2}^{(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N^{2}},

(61c)

\displaystyle e^{2a^{(t)}_{c,0}}-e^{2a^{(t^{\prime})}_{c,0}}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(61d)

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}-e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\geq\max\left\{\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),0\right\},

(61e)

\displaystyle|a_{p,0}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\ln k,\quad|a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}|\lesssim\ln k,

(61f)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(61g)

\displaystyle a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim a_{q,\times}^{(t)},

(61h)

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\asymp a_{q,\times}^{(t)},

(61i)

\displaystyle\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)-\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(61j)

\displaystyle-\left(a_{q,0}^{(t)}-a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}}\left(\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)-\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)\right),

(61k)

\displaystyle\xi_{1,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim a_{q,\times}^{(t)}.

(61l)

Moreover, for all
t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T_{1,1}
:

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}\lesssim\frac{\ln k}{k^{2}},\quad|a_{p,2}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\ln k}{k^{2}},

(62a)

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\eta(t-t^{\prime})}{k^{2}},

(62b)

\displaystyle\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)-\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle\asymp a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})},

(62c)

\displaystyle a_{q,0}^{(t)}-a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)+C.

(62d)

For any
t^{\prime},t
such that
T_{1,1}+1\leq t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T_{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\eta(t-t^{\prime})}{k^{5}},

(63)

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\ln^{2}k.

(64)

For any
t^{\prime},t
such that
T_{1,1}+1\leq t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T_{1,2}
, we have

\displaystyle-\frac{\ln k}{k^{2}}\lesssim a_{p,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 1+O\left(\frac{\ln k}{k^{2}}\right),

(65a)

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,0}^{(t)}}-e^{2a_{c,0}^{(t^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)\left(a_{q,0}^{(t)}-a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),

(65b)

\displaystyle 0\leq a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 2.1\ln k,

(65c)

\displaystyle a_{q,0}^{(t)}+a_{q,\times}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\asymp\ln k.

(65d)

For any
t^{\prime},t
such that
T_{1,2}+1\leq t^{\prime}\leq t\leq T_{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle-C\leq a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 6.2\ln k,

(66a)

\displaystyle a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq 1.9\ln k-1,

(66b)

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq 2.425\ln k,

(66c)

\displaystyle a_{p,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{k}a_{p,1}^{(t)},

(66d)

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,0}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\asymp\ln^{2}k,

(66e)

\displaystyle 5.9\ln k\leq a_{q,0}^{(t)}+a_{q,\times}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq 20.4\ln k.

(66f)

When
t=T_{1/2}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{b,1}^{(T_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N},

(67a)

\displaystyle-\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N}\lesssim a_{b,0}^{(T_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{k^{2}\ln^{2}k}{N},

(67b)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k^{3.05}}\lesssim 1-R\left(\pi^{(T_{1/2})};{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{k^{1.01}},

(67c)

\displaystyle\forall 2\leq l\leq L:\quad R\left(\pi^{(T_{1/2})};{\mathcal{P}}_{l}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{k},

(67d)

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(T_{1/2})}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}

\displaystyle\geq 3.9\ln k.

(67e)

The proof of Lemma
2
is postponed to Appendix
A.3.2
. As a consequence of Lemma
2
, we have

\displaystyle T_{1/2}\lesssim\frac{k^{6}\ln k}{\eta}.

(68)

Phase 1.2:
T_{1/2}+1\leq t\leq T_{1}
.

At Phase 1.2, the effect of matrix
A_{B}
becomes non-negligible. Specifically, its main diagonal
a_{b,1}
will monotonically increase,
a_{b,0}
is negative at first, and then becomes positive if
\epsilon
is small enough (smaller than
\frac{1}{k^{9/4}N\exp\left(k^{1.3}\sqrt{N}\right)}
), and
a_{b,2}
stays small. Same as in Phase 1.1,
a_{c,0}
and
a_{c,1}
will monotonically increase, but
a_{c,0}
’s increase becomes much slower and
e^{2a_{c,0}}
stays on the order
\ln^{2}k
after Phase 1.1.
a_{p,1}
will monotonically increase,
a_{p,2}\approx\frac{a_{p,1}}{k-1}
after Phase 1.1, and
a_{p,0},a_{p,\texttt{up}}
are no larger than
a_{p,2}
in magnitude.
a_{q,0}
,
a_{q,\times}
increase overall, and has the same magnitude as
a_{p,2}
.
The failure probability will keep decreasing until it’s bounded by
\epsilon^{1.05}
at step
T_{1}
.

To formalize the above description, we define

\displaystyle T_{1,3}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sup\left\{t\in{\mathbb{N}}:a_{p,1}^{(t)}\leq 21k\ln k\right\},

(69a)

\displaystyle T_{1,4}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\inf\left\{t\in{\mathbb{N}}:e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}\geq\frac{k}{\ln k}\right\},

(69b)

and for all
h\in[k]
, define

\displaystyle\mu_{2h-1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+\frac{h-1}{k}a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}+(h-1)a_{p,2}^{(t)}e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+(h-1)e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(h-1)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}},

(70a)

\displaystyle\mu_{2h}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+\frac{h-1}{k}a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}+ha_{p,2}^{(t)}e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+(2h-1)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}},

(70b)

and

\displaystyle r_{\texttt{down}_{\times}}^{(t)}\coloneqq\frac{e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}}{e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}+(k-1)e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+(k-1)e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}}.

(71)

We state the training dynamics of Phase 1.2 in Lemma
3
.

Lemma 3
.

Under balanced goal distribution (
19
), Assumption
1
, for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[T_{1/2},T_{1}]
with
t^{\prime}\leq t
, there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that we have

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}-e^{a_{b,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(72a)

\displaystyle a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\asymp a_{q,\times}^{(t)}\asymp a_{p,2}^{(t)},

(72b)

\displaystyle e^{2a^{(t)}_{c,0}}-e^{2a^{(t^{\prime})}_{c,0}}

\displaystyle\geq 0,\quad e^{2a^{(t)}_{c,0}}\asymp\ln^{2}k,

(72c)

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}-e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}

\displaystyle\geq\max\left\{(2+o(1))\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),0\right\},

(72d)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

(72e)

\displaystyle\left|a_{p,0}^{(t)}-a_{p,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)-\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),

(72f)

\displaystyle\left|a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}-a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t^{\prime})}\right|

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)-\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}+a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),

(72g)

\displaystyle a_{p,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+O\left(1/k^{2}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}}{k-1}.

(72h)

For
t,t^{\prime}\in[T_{1/2}+1,T_{1,3}]
,
t^{\prime}\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq 3.9\ln k.

(73a)

\displaystyle a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq 1.9\ln k-2,

(73b)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\eta(t-t^{\prime})}{k^{12.5}},

(73c)

\displaystyle a_{b,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\asymp\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N},

(73d)

\displaystyle-\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N}\lesssim a_{b,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim-\frac{k^{2}\ln^{2}k}{N}.

(73e)

For
t\in[T_{1,3}+1,T_{1,4}]
,
t^{\prime}\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}\leq k^{9/4}\sqrt{{N}},

(74a)

\displaystyle\min\left\{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2}^{(t)}-\ln k,a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2(k-1)}^{(t)}+\ln k\right\}+\frac{2}{k}a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq r_{\texttt{down}_{\times}}^{(t)}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)+\ln k,

(74b)

\displaystyle r_{\texttt{down}_{\times}}^{(t)}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)+0.1\sqrt{a_{p,1}^{(t)}/k}+5\ln k

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2}^{(t)},a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2(k-1)}^{(t)}\right\},

(74c)

\displaystyle r_{\texttt{down}_{\times}}^{(t)}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)+2.1\ln k

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)a_{q,0}^{(t)}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k},

(74d)

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)a_{q,0}^{(t)}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}+0.1\sqrt{a_{p,1}^{(t)}/k}+3\ln k

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2}^{(t)},a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2(k-1)}^{(t)}\right\},

(74e)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}-1

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}\right)^{2}}{N}\gtrsim k^{2}\left(e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}-1\right),

(74f)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},

(74g)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}+\frac{1}{k^{8}N}a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\left(e^{a_{b,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}+\frac{1}{k^{8}N}a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1+o(1)}{N}\frac{ka_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}{3}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),

(74h)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{k^{6.5}}{\ln k},

(74i)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\eta(t-t^{\prime})}{\exp\left(k^{1.3}\sqrt{N}\right)},

(74j)

For
t\in[T_{1,4}+1,T_{1}]
, we have

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k}{\ln k},

(75a)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k^{2}\sqrt{N}}{\ln k},

(75b)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k^{5}}{\ln^{2}k},

(75c)

\displaystyle\min\left\{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2}^{(t)}-\ln k,a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2(k-1)}^{(t)}+\ln k\right\}+\frac{2}{k}a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\geq r_{\texttt{down}_{\times}}^{(t)}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)-0.1\sqrt{a_{p,1}^{(t)}/k}+\ln k,

(75d)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k-1}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)+2.1\ln k

\displaystyle\geq\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)a_{q,0}^{(t)}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k},

(75e)

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)a_{q,0}^{(t)}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}+0.1\sqrt{a_{p,1}^{(t)}/k}+3\ln k

\displaystyle\geq\min\left\{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2}^{(t)},a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-\mu_{2(k-1)}^{(t)}\right\},

(75f)

\displaystyle e^{\frac{N}{N-1}a_{b,1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}\right)^{2}}{N}\gtrsim k^{2}e^{\frac{N}{N-1}a_{b,0}^{(t)}},

(75g)

\displaystyle\text{If }a_{p,1}^{(t)}\geq Nk^{3}:\quad e^{a_{b,0}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}\right)^{1-\frac{6}{N-1}}}{Nk},

(75h)

\displaystyle(1+o(1))\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}\right)^{-\frac{6}{N-1}}\leq e^{a_{b,2}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle<1.

(75i)

When
t=T_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(T_{1})}

\displaystyle\asymp k\ln(1/\epsilon),

(76a)

\displaystyle\left(k-1+o\left(\frac{1}{\ln(1/\epsilon)}\right)\right)\ln\left(\frac{1}{k\epsilon^{1.05}}-(k-1)\right)

\displaystyle\leq a_{p,1}^{(T_{1})}+a_{p,2}^{(T_{1})}\leq(k+C)\ln(k^{2}/\epsilon^{1.05}),

(76b)

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)a_{q,0}^{(T_{1})}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(T_{1})}}{k}

\displaystyle\geq\ln\left(\frac{1}{k\epsilon^{1.05}}-\frac{1}{k}\right)

(76c)

\displaystyle\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)a_{q,\times}^{(T_{1})}+\frac{a_{p,0}^{(T_{1})}}{k}

\displaystyle\geq\ln\left(\frac{(k-1)^{2}}{k\epsilon^{1.05}}-(k-1)\right)+\frac{C}{\ln(1/\epsilon)},

(76d)

\displaystyle a_{c,1}^{(T_{1})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\ln^{2}\left(1/\epsilon\right)

(76e)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,0}^{(T_{1})}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{1}{N}\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1-\frac{6}{N-1}},

(76f)

\displaystyle e^{a_{b,1}^{(T_{1})}}\gtrsim\frac{k^{2}}{N^{2}}\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{3-\frac{6}{N-1}}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{k^{2}}{N}\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1-\frac{5}{N}}e^{a_{b,0}^{(T_{1})}},

(76g)

\displaystyle(1+o(1))\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{-\frac{6}{N-1}}\lesssim e^{a_{b,2}^{(T_{1})}}

\displaystyle\leq\min\left\{1,(1+o(1))\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{-\frac{4}{N-1}+\frac{12}{N(N-1)}}\right\},

(76h)

\displaystyle\forall 2\leq l\leq L:\quad R\left(\pi^{(T_{1})};{\mathcal{P}}_{l}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1+o(1)}{k^{l-1}},

(76i)

and

\displaystyle T_{1}\lesssim\frac{1}{\eta\epsilon^{1.1}}.

(77)

The proof of Lemma
3
is postponed to Appendix
A.3.5
. The statement tracks the most stringent precision regime in Theorem
5
. For a larger target precision
\epsilon
, the depth-1 success criterion may already be met before the later Phase 1.2 thresholds, such as
T_{1,4}
, are reached. This is the regime reflected in Figure
5
and Figure
7
: training can stop while
a_{b,0}
is still slightly negative or close to zero, even though the analysis would make it positive after sufficiently long depth-1 training. The resulting policy still learns the same search mechanism. It simply realizes it with a slightly different finite-precision balance between
A_{B}
and
A_{P}
; in particular, during depth-2 curriculum training,
A_{P}
can converge to adjusted values, with
a_{p,\texttt{up}}
growing to the scale needed for backtracking in the search policy.

A.2.2
Depth-2 curriculum training

The main reason of poor depth generalization after depth-1 curriculum training is that
a_{p,2}
is too large. From the proof of (
76i
) in Lemma
3
we can see that this causes the agent not to go up with high probability at a non-leaf and non-root node, after all its children have been visited.
We’ll show that at depth-2 curriculum training,
a_{p,1}
keeps monotonically increasing,
a_{p,2}=\frac{a_{p,1}-a_{p,3}}{k-1}
(c.f. (
52d
)) keeps decreasing until it has the right proportion to
a_{p,1}
. The changes of
a_{p,0}
and
a_{p,\texttt{up}}
are relatively small during depth-2 curriculum training.
Below we formally state the training dynamics of depth-2 curriculum training in Lemma
4
. Recall we fix
B,C,Q
to be
B^{(T_{1})},C^{(T_{1})},Q^{(T_{1})}
after depth-1 curriculum training and only train
\overline{P}
starting from
\overline{P}^{(T_{1})}
.

Lemma 4
.

Under balanced goal distribution (
19
), Assumption
1
, for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
with
t^{\prime}\leq t
, we have

\displaystyle|a_{p,0}^{(t)}-a_{p,0}^{(T_{1})}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{a_{q,0}}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+o(1)}},

(78a)

\displaystyle|a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}-a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(T_{1})}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{a_{q,0}}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{3+o(1)}},

(78b)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\gtrsim\frac{\epsilon^{1.1}}{k^{L}}(\eta(t-t^{\prime})),

(78c)

\displaystyle-\frac{a_{q,0}}{k}\lesssim a_{p,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\lesssim ka_{q,0},

(78d)

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\asymp ka_{q,0}.

(78e)

And there exists an absolute constant
C\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that for

\displaystyle T_{2,1}\coloneqq\inf_{t}\left\{t\in[T_{1}+1,T_{2}]:a_{p,3}^{(t)}\geq\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}}{2}+k\left(1+\frac{C}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right\},

(79)

for all
t,t^{\prime}\in[T_{1},T_{2,1}]
with
t^{\prime}\leq t
:

\displaystyle a_{p,3}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t^{\prime})}\geq(1+o(1))k\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,1}^{(t^{\prime})}\right),

(80)

and for all
t\in[T_{2,1}+1,T_{2}]
:

\displaystyle a_{p,3}^{(t)}=\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}}{2}+k\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}.

(81a)

When
t=T_{2}
, there exists a constant
c_{1},c_{2},c_{3},c_{4}\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{p,3}^{(T_{2})}}{k}-\left(1-\frac{c_{1}}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}

\displaystyle\geq\ln\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\epsilon^{1.02}}\right),

(81b)

\displaystyle\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{2})}}{k}

\displaystyle\geq 2\ln\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\epsilon^{1.02}}\right)+\frac{c_{2}}{\ln^{2}k}a_{q,0},

(81c)

\displaystyle\frac{a_{p,3}^{(T_{2})}}{k}-\left(1-\frac{c_{3}}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}

\displaystyle\leq\ln\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}\right),

(81d)

\displaystyle\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{2})}}{k}

\displaystyle\leq 2\ln\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}\right)+\frac{c_{4}}{\ln^{2}k}a_{q,0}.

(81e)

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle T_{2}-T_{1}\lesssim\frac{k^{L}}{\eta\epsilon^{1.1}}.

(82)

For any full
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
(that may not be a perfect
k
-ary tree) with depth
l\in[L]
and any goal node
g({\mathcal{T}})
,

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(T_{2})},({\mathcal{T}},g({\mathcal{T}})))\geq 1-\epsilon.

(83)

The proof of Lemma
4
is postponed to Appendix
A.3.7
. Theorem
5
follows directly from Lemma
2
, Lemma
3
and Lemma
4
.

A.3
Proof of Key Lemmas

A.3.1
Proof of Lemma
1

In the proof, for notation simplicity, we may drop superscript
t
when it’s clear from the context.

Step 1: compute
\delta B
,
\delta C
,
\delta P
,
\delta Q
.

We first compute
\delta B
,
\delta C
,
\delta P
,
\delta Q
by chain rule.
Note that

d\phi_{h}=(dP)E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}
,
and thus

d\log\pi_{h}(a_{h})=\delta_{h}^{\top}d\phi_{h}=\delta_{h}^{\top}(dP)E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}=\textnormal{tr}\left(\delta_{h}(dP)E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}\right)=\textnormal{tr}\left((dP)E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}\delta_{h}^{\top}\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{P}\log\pi_{h}(a_{h})=\delta_{h}\left(E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}\right)^{\top},

(84)

and thus by (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\delta P=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}\delta_{h}\left(E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}\right)^{\top}\right],

(85)

where
\widehat{G}_{h}
is defined in (
A.2
). By the same argument, we have

\displaystyle\delta Q=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}\delta_{h}\left(E_{c}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{c}\right)^{\top}\right].

(86)

Let the softmax Jacobians be

\displaystyle J_{h}^{n}\coloneqq\diag(\alpha_{h}^{n})-\alpha_{h}^{n}(\alpha_{h}^{n})^{\top},\quad J_{h}^{c}\coloneqq\diag(\alpha_{h}^{c})-\alpha_{h}^{c}(\alpha_{h}^{c})^{\top},

then we have

\displaystyle d\log\pi_{h}(a_{h})=\delta_{h}^{\top}d\phi_{h}=\delta_{h}^{\top}PE_{a}^{(h)}J_{h}^{n}\left(E_{n}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}(dB)\overline{n}_{h}=\textnormal{tr}\left(\overline{n}_{h}\delta_{h}^{\top}PE_{a}^{(h)}J_{h}^{n}\left(E_{n}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}(dB)\right).

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{B}\log\pi_{h}(a_{h})=E_{n}^{(h)}J_{h}^{n}\left(E_{a}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}P^{\top}\delta_{h}\overline{n}_{h}^{\top},

(87)

and thus by (
3.1
), we have

\displaystyle\delta B=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}E_{n}^{(h)}J_{h}^{n}\left(E_{a}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}P^{\top}\delta_{h}\overline{n}_{h}^{\top}\right].

(88)

By the same argument, we have

\displaystyle\delta C=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}E_{c}^{(h)}J_{h}^{c}\left(E_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}Q^{\top}\delta_{h}\overline{c}_{h}^{\top}\right].

(89)

Step 2: compute
\delta A_{B}
,
\delta A_{C}
,
\delta A_{P}
,
\delta A_{Q}
.

We write the per-step selector factorizations

\displaystyle E_{n}^{(h)}=US_{n}^{(h)},\quad E_{a}^{(h)}=VS_{a}^{(h)},\quad E_{c}^{(h)}=ZS_{c}^{(h)},

(90)

where each column of
S_{n}^{(h)}
,
S_{a}^{(h)}
,
S_{c}^{(h)}
is a one-hot selector vector that has 1 at the position of the corresponding node, action, or label and 0 otherwise.
Then we have

\displaystyle\delta A_{P}

\displaystyle=\delta PV\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_P}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}\delta_{h}\left(E_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}\right)^{\top}\right]V\overset{\eqref{eq:selector_factorizations}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}\delta_{h}\left(\alpha_{h}^{n}\right)^{\top}\left(S_{a}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}\right],

(91)

\displaystyle\delta A_{Q}

\displaystyle=\delta QZ\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_Q}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}\delta_{h}\left(E_{c}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{c}\right)^{\top}\right]Z\overset{\eqref{eq:selector_factorizations}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}\delta_{h}\left(\alpha_{h}^{c}\right)^{\top}\left(S_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}\right],

(92)

\displaystyle\delta A_{B}

\displaystyle=U^{\top}\delta BU\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_B}}{=}U^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}E_{n}^{(h)}J_{h}^{n}\left(E_{a}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}P^{\top}\delta_{h}\overline{n}_{h}^{\top}\right]U\overset{\eqref{eq:selector_factorizations}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}S_{n}^{(h)}J_{h}^{n}\left(S_{a}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}A_{P}^{\top}\delta_{h}e_{n_{h}}^{\top}\right],

(93)

\displaystyle\delta A_{C}

\displaystyle=Z^{\top}\delta CZ\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_C}}{=}Z^{\top}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}E_{c}^{(h)}J_{h}^{c}\left(E_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}Q^{\top}\delta_{h}\overline{c}_{h}^{\top}\right]Z\overset{\eqref{eq:selector_factorizations}}{=}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}S_{c}^{(h)}J_{h}^{c}\left(S_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}A_{Q}^{\top}\delta_{h}e_{c_{h}}^{\top}\right],

(94)

where
e_{n_{h}}\in\mathbb{R}^{N+1}
and
e_{c_{h}}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
are the one-hot vectors that have 1 at the position of
n_{h}
and
c_{h}
respectively and 0 otherwise.

We next simplify the matrix expressions and their gradients by exploiting the specific structure of the gradient matrices. To do so, we first introduce some notation. We define the (pre-softmax) logits as

\displaystyle\phi_{h}\coloneqq PE_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}+QE_{c}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{c}

(95)

with

\displaystyle\alpha_{h}^{n}\coloneqq\mathsf{softmax}\left(\left(E_{n}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}B\overline{n}_{h}\right),\quad\alpha_{h}^{c}\coloneqq\mathsf{softmax}\left(\left(E_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}C\overline{c}_{h}\right).

(96)

Then by (
11
), we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}=\mathsf{softmax}(\phi_{h})=\mathsf{softmax}\left(PE_{a}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{n}+QE_{c}^{(h)}\alpha_{h}^{c}\right).

(97)

We also define

\displaystyle\delta_{h}\coloneqq e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h},

(98)

where
e_{a_{h}}
is the one-hot vector that has 1 at the position of
a_{h}
and 0 otherwise.

To better align with the node/action/label numbering, we let
A_{B},A_{C}
be 0-indexed,
A_{P},A_{Q}
’s column indices be 0-indexed, and
A_{P},A_{Q}
’s row indices be 1-indexed, and we let
\times
and
\checkmark
denote index 1 and 2 respectively, e.g.,
A_{C}(0,\times)=A_{C}(0,1)
,
e_{\times}=e_{1}
. We also let
up
denote index
k+1
. For each
h\in[H]
, we define

\displaystyle p_{h,\times}^{c}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\},\quad p_{h,0}^{c}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=0\}.

(99)

Then since
c_{h}\in\{0,\times\}
for all
h\in[H]
, we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in[H]:\quad p_{h,\times}^{c}+p_{h,0}^{c}=1.

(100)

Analogously, we define

\displaystyle p_{h,j}^{a}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{n}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{a_{i-1}=j\},\,\,\forall j\in\{0,1,\cdots,k+1\},

(101a)

\displaystyle p_{h,j}^{n}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{n}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{n_{i-1}=j\},\,\,\forall j\in\{0,1,\cdots,N\},

(101b)

which satisfies

\displaystyle\forall h\in[H]:\quad\sum_{j=0}^{k+1}p_{h,j}^{a}=1,\quad\sum_{j=0}^{N}p_{h,j}^{n}=1.

(102)

First, since once goal is found, i.e.,
\checkmark
is observed, the episode terminates and the gradient matrices are not updated anymore. Therefore, the input matrix
E_{c}^{(h)}
won’t contain any
z_{2}
(the embedding of label
\checkmark
) for any
h\in[H]
. Thus by (
94
) and our zero initialization, we have

\displaystyle\forall t\in{\mathbb{N}}:\quad\delta A_{C}^{(t)}(\checkmark,:)=\delta A_{C}^{(t)}(:,\checkmark)=0\quad\text{and}\quad A_{C}^{(t)}(\checkmark,:)=A_{C}^{(t)}(:,\checkmark)=0.

(103)

For the same reason, by (
92
), we have

\displaystyle\forall t\in{\mathbb{N}}:\quad\delta A_{Q}^{(t)}(:,\checkmark)=0\quad\text{and}\quad A_{Q}^{(t)}(:,\checkmark)=0.

(104)

Further, since
n_{h}\neq 0
for all
h\in[H]
, by (
93
), we have

\displaystyle\forall t\in{\mathbb{N}}:\quad\delta A_{B}^{(t)}(:,0)=0\quad\text{and}\quad A_{B}^{(t)}(:,0)=0.

(105)

Moreover, by (
94
), we have

\displaystyle 1^{\top}\delta A_{C}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}1^{\top}S_{c}^{(h)}J_{h}^{c}\left(S_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}A_{Q}^{\top}\delta_{h}e_{c_{h}}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\widehat{G}_{h}1^{\top}J_{h}^{c}\left(S_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}A_{Q}^{\top}\delta_{h}e_{c_{h}}^{\top}\right]=0,

(106)

where the first equality uses the fact that
1^{\top}S_{c}^{(h)}=1^{\top}
and the second equality uses the fact that
1^{\top}J_{h}^{c}=0
. This combined with (
103
) and our zero initialization suggests that

\displaystyle\forall t\in{\mathbb{N}},j\in\{0,1\}:\quad\delta A_{C}^{(t)}(0,j)=-\delta A_{C}^{(t)}(1,j)\quad\text{and}\quad A_{C}^{(t)}(0,j)=-A_{C}^{(t)}(1,j).

(107)

The equations (
103
) and (
107
) allow us to express
A_{C}^{(t)}
and
\delta A_{C}^{(t)}
as in (
46
).
Similarly, we can further compute that each column of other 3 matrices also sum up to 0 using (
93
), (
91
), (
92
), i.e.,

\displaystyle 1^{\top}\delta A_{M}=0\quad\text{for }M\in\{B,P,Q\}.

(108)

This and (
A.3.1
) combined with our zero initialization yields

\displaystyle\forall t\in{\mathbb{N}}:\quad 1^{\top}\delta A_{M}^{(t)}=0,\quad\text{for }M\in\{B,C,P,Q\};\quad 1^{\top}A_{M}^{(t)}=0,\quad\text{for }M\in\{B,C,Q\}.

(109)

By exploiting the symmetry of the data distribution and using (
109
), we could represent
A_{P}
and
A_{Q}
as in (
51
) and (
53
), and we can also obtain (
52a
).

We next compute
\delta a_{c,0}
and
\delta a_{c,1}
in (
46
).
By (
94
), we have

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,1}

\displaystyle=e_{1}^{\top}\delta A_{C}e_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left((\mathbbm{1}\{c_{1}=\times\},\cdots,\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\})\right)\left(\diag\{\alpha_{h}^{c}\}-\alpha_{h}^{c}(\alpha_{h}^{c})^{\top}\right)\left(S_{c}^{(h)}\right)^{\top}A_{Q}^{\top}(e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h})\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\widehat{G}_{h}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Bigg[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\Bigg\{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}\right)A_{Q}(:,\times)^{\top}-\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}A_{Q}(:,\times)^{\top}+\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=0\}A_{Q}(:,0)^{\top}\right)\Bigg\}(e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h})\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\widehat{G}_{h}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=0\}\right)\left(A_{Q}(:,\times)-A_{Q}(:,0)\right)^{\top}(e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h})\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\widehat{G}_{h}\right].

(110)

Combining (
99
), we have

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}p_{h,\times}^{c}p_{h,0}^{c}\left(A_{Q}(:,\times)-A_{Q}(:,0)\right)^{\top}(e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h})\widehat{G}_{h}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}p_{h,\times}^{c}p_{h,0}^{c}\left(A_{Q}(:,\times)-A_{Q}(:,0)\right)^{\top}\left(\pi_{h}\odot\left(Q_{h}-G_{h}{\bm{1}}\right)\right)\right].

(111)

Similarly, we can compute that

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,0}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=0\}\right)\left(\sum_{i=1}^{h}\alpha_{h}^{c}(i)\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}\right)\left(A_{Q}(:,0)-A_{Q}(:,\times)\right)^{\top}(e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h})\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0\}\widehat{G}_{h}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0\}p_{h,0}^{c}p_{h,\times}^{c}\left(A_{Q}(:,0)-A_{Q}(:,\times)\right)^{\top}(e_{a_{h}}-\pi_{h})\widehat{G}_{h}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0\}p_{h,0}^{c}p_{h,\times}^{c}\left(A_{Q}(:,0)-A_{Q}(:,\times)\right)^{\top}\left(\pi_{h}\odot\left(Q_{h}-G_{h}{\bm{1}}\right)\right)\right].

From (

and

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,1}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}p_{h,0}^{c}p_{h,\times}^{c}\left(a_{q,\times}+a_{q,0}\right)\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})(Q_{h}(\texttt{up})-G_{h})\right]

where the last relation uses the fact that

Further note that

\frac{(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}},\,\,\text{if }c_{h}=0.\end{cases}

(115)

Plugging (

(117a)

\displaystyle\delta a_{b,1}

(117b)

). We can further simplify

\displaystyle\delta a_{b,0}

0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

where

Policy computation.

We let

\displaystyle\varphi_{h}

\displaystyle\coloneqq A_{P}(:,0)e^{a_{b,0}}+\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}A_{P}(:,j)e^{a_{b,1}}

\displaystyle\qquad+\left(N_{h}(\texttt{up})A_{P}(:,\texttt{up})+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(N_{h}(i)-\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\right)A_{P}(:,i)\right)e^{a_{b,2}}.

(126)

Then by (
11
), under Assumption
1
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{softmax}\Bigg(\frac{\varphi_{h}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}}}+\begin{cases}\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}A_{Q}(:,\times)+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}A_{Q}(:,0)}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=0\\
\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}}A_{Q}(:,\times)+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}}A_{Q}(:,0)}{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times\end{cases}\Bigg).

(127)

By (
127
) and (
51
), we have

\displaystyle\varphi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=0,

(128a)

\displaystyle\forall m\in[k]:\quad\varphi_{h}(m)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{p,0}}{k}e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|a_{p,2}e^{a_{b,1}}-\mathbbm{1}\{m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\left((a_{p,1}+a_{p,2})(e^{a_{b,1}}-e^{a_{b,2}})\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(N_{h}(\texttt{up})\frac{a_{p,\texttt{up}}}{k}-N_{h}(m)a_{p,1}+(h-1-N_{h}(\texttt{up})-N_{h}(m)-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)a_{p,2}\right)e^{a_{b,2}}.

(128b)

The above two expressions give (
1
). (
51
) together with (
127
) gives (
57
).

A.3.2
Proof of Lemma
2

We show by induction.
First, by our zero initialization, Lemma
2
holds for
t=0
. We make the following induction hypothesis:

Induction Hypothesis I:
For all
t\leq s-1
(
s\in[T_{1/2}]
), the relations in Lemma
2
hold.

Below we show Lemma
2
holds for
t=s
under Induction Hypothesis I. To do so, we first simplify the gradients for
l=1
.
We define events

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{1,h}\coloneqq\left\{\text{the environment has not terminated at step }h,\text{ and the tree depth }l=1\right\}.

(129)

Recall we define
\widehat{G}_{h}
in (
A.2
). We let
V_{1,h}^{\pi}
denote the expectation of
\widehat{G}_{h}
conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,h}
under policy
\pi
:

\displaystyle V_{1,h}^{\pi}\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}^{\pi}[\widehat{G}_{h}|{\mathcal{E}}_{1,h}].

(130)

We let
V_{1,h}^{(t)}\coloneqq V_{1,h}^{\pi^{(t)}}
. Note that
V_{1,h}^{(t)}
is the same for any possible
s_{h}
conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,h}
and
\pi_{h}^{(t)}
by symmetry under the balanced case. We let
\pi_{1,h}^{(t)}(i)
denote
\pi_{h}^{(t)}(i)
conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,h}
and
\pi_{h}^{(t)}
for any
i\in[k+1]
.
Then by symmetry, for all odd steps
2h-1
(
h\in[k]
) (where the agent is at the root node when
l=1
), we have
\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(i)
are equal for any
i\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{2h-1,\times}
for any
t\in{\mathbb{N}}
.
We let
\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down})\coloneqq\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(i)
for any
i\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{2h-1,\times}
. Similarly,
\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(i)
are also equal for any
i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2h-1,\times}
, and we let
\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})\coloneqq\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(i)
for any
i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2h-1,\times}
. We also let

\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times}^{0})\coloneqq\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(i)\qquad\mbox{for any}~{i\in[k]\setminus\left({\mathcal{A}}_{2h-1,\times}\cup\{a_{2h-1}\}\right)}

and

\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times}^{1})\coloneqq\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(i)\qquad\mbox{for any}~{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2h-1,\times}\cup\{a_{2h-1}\}},

whose values are also independent of
s_{2h}
conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}
. We let
{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi}({\mathcal{E}}_{1,h})
denote the probability of event
{\mathcal{E}}_{1,h}
happening under policy
\pi
. We let
{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}({\mathcal{E}}_{1,h})\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi^{(t)}}({\mathcal{E}}_{1,h})
for notation simplicity.

Gradients/policy simplification for Phase 1.1.

By Induction Hypothesis I, using the above notation we define, we could further simplify the gradients and policy expressions in Lemma
1
when
l=1
. We summarize the results in the following lemma.

Lemma 5
.

Under Induction Hypothesis I, we have for any
0\leq t\leq s-1
:

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\pi_{1,1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\bigg(\frac{1}{1+\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(t)}}}\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-\frac{1}{1+e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)\bigg)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131a)

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\bigg(\frac{1}{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)-\frac{1}{1+\frac{h+1}{h}e^{2a_{c,0}^{(t)}}}\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\bigg)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131b)

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\frac{h(h+1)}{\left(he^{-a_{c,0}^{(t)}}+(h+1)e^{a_{c,0}^{(t)}}\right)^{2}}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131c)

\displaystyle\delta a_{c,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\frac{1}{\left(e^{a_{c,1}^{(t)}}+e^{-a_{c,1}^{(t)}}\right)^{2}}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right)(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}))V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131d)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\left(-\pi_{1,1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,1}^{(t)}+\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(\frac{1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})}{2h}-\frac{\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})}{2h+1}\right)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}\right),

(131e)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{1}{k}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(\frac{1}{2}\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times}^{1})+\frac{h}{2h+1}\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})\right)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131f)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{1}{k}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(\frac{1}{2}\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)-\frac{h}{2h+1}\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131g)

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=-(1+o(1))\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(\frac{h-1}{2h}\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)-\frac{h}{2h+1}\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131h)

\displaystyle\delta a_{b,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{N}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})I_{h,0}^{(t)}V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131i)

\displaystyle\delta a_{b,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{N}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})I_{h,1}^{(t)}V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)},

(131j)

where

\displaystyle I_{h,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{1}{4h^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{2h-1}{k}a_{p,0}^{(t)}+ha_{p,2}^{(t)}+\frac{h-1}{k}a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}\right)\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)-\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)h\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times}^{1})\right)

\displaystyle\quad\,-\frac{h}{(2h+1)^{2}}\left(\left(\frac{1}{k}\left(2a_{p,0}^{(t)}+a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}\right)+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})+\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})\right),

(132a)

\displaystyle I_{h,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{h}{(2h+1)^{2}}\bigg[(h+1)\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})+\left(\frac{1}{k}\left(a_{p,0}^{(t)}-ha_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}\right)+(h+1)a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\bigg].

(132b)

For all
\forall h\in[k]
,
i\in[k]
, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(t)}-\frac{h-1}{2h-1}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)}{(h-1)\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{2h-1}\right)+k-h+1+\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(t)}-\frac{h-1}{2h-1}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)},

(133a)

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)}{h\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{2h}\right)+k-h+\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)},

(133b)

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(i)

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{N_{2h-1}(i)}{2h-1}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\right)}{(h-1)\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{2h-1}\right)+k-h+1+\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(t)}-\frac{h-1}{2h-1}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)},

(133c)

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(i)

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{\exp\left(-\frac{N_{2h}(i)}{2h}\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\right)}{h\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{2h}\right)+k-h+\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)},

(133d)

where

\displaystyle\xi_{2h-1}^{(t)}\coloneqq-\frac{a_{q,0}^{(t)}-\frac{h-1}{h}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(t)}}a_{q,\times}^{(t)}}{1+\frac{h-1}{h}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(t)}}},\quad\xi_{2h}^{(t)}\coloneqq\frac{a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}a_{q,0}^{(t)}}{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}.

(134)

The proof of Lemma
5
is postponed to Appendix
A.3.3
.

Define the ratios

\displaystyle\nu_{h}^{(t)}\coloneqq\frac{\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})}{\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})},\quad r_{h}^{(t)}\coloneqq\frac{\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(t)}-\frac{h-1}{2h-1}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)}{\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{2}a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)}.

(135)

We next divide Phase 1.1 into two cases: (i)
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
and (ii)
T_{1,1}+1\leq s-1\leq T_{1/2}
.

Case 1:
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
.

(
131f
) and Induction Hypothesis I imply that (
61g
) holds at step
s-1
.
By Induction Hypothesis I, we can simplify the policy expressions (
133a
), (
133b
), (
133c
), (

) when
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
as follows:

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k],i\in[k]:\quad\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(s-1)}\right)}{k+\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(s-1)}\right)},

(136a)

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(s-1)}\right)}{k+\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(s-1)}\right)},

(136b)

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s-1)}(i)

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{1}{k+\exp\left(\xi_{2h-1}^{(s-1)}\right)},

(136c)

\displaystyle\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(i)

\displaystyle=\left(1+o(1)\right)\frac{1}{k+\exp\left(\xi_{2h}^{(s-1)}\right)}.

(136d)

Then by (
136a
), (
136b
), (
62c
) and (
62a
) in Induction Hypothesis I, we have

\displaystyle r_{h}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\exp\Bigg(\underbrace{-\frac{1-\frac{h-1}{h}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}}{\left(1+\frac{h-1}{h}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}\right)\left(1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}\right)}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\right)}_{\leq 0}\Bigg)\leq 1+o(1).

(137)

Note that if
a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\gtrsim\ln k
, there exists
s^{\prime}<s
such that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{3}a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\leq a_{q,\times}^{(s^{\prime})}\leq\frac{1}{2}a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)},

and by (

), (

), we have

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}\geq e^{2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}-e^{2a_{c,1}^{(s^{\prime})}}\gtrsim\left(a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\right)^{2}.

(138)

Thus by (
61h
) in Induction Hypothesis I, we have

\displaystyle\text{If }a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\gtrsim\ln k:\quad\xi_{2h}^{(s-1)}=\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}.

(139)

Thus by the definition of
T_{1,1}
in (
60a
) and (
139
), we have

\displaystyle\forall s-1\leq T_{1,1}:\quad\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{1+o(1)}{2}.

(140)

By (
136b
) and (
137
), we have when
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
:

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\leq(1+o(1))\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})=(1+o(1))\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up}).

(141)

If
\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
, then by (
141
), we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}},\quad\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}.

(142)

Thus by (
131b
), we have

\displaystyle\text{when $\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}$:}\quad\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(s-1)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}.

(143)

If
\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
, since
\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{1+o(1)}{2}
(c.f. (

)),
we have

\displaystyle\xi_{2}^{(s-1)}\overset{\eqref{eq:xi_h_phase1}}{=}\frac{a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}-e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}}{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}}\asymp\ln k.

By (
62c
) and (
61l
) Induction Hypothesis I, we have

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\asymp a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\gtrsim\xi_{2}^{(s-1)}\asymp\ln k.

(144)

Note that when
a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\gtrsim 1
, by (

) in Induction Hypothesis I, we have

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}\geq C\text{ for some constant }C>1.

(145)

Thus by (

), (
145
), (

), we have when
\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\gtrsim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
,

\displaystyle\nu_{h}^{(s-1)}\leq\frac{1}{k^{c_{1}}}\text{ for some constant }c_{1}>0.

(146)

By (
142
), (
146
) and (
141
), we know that

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{1}{k^{c}}\text{ for some constant }c>0.

(147)

Combining (
147
) with (
143
), we have for any
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
:

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(s-1)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\right)V_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}.

(148)

By comparing (
131f
) and (

), we have

\displaystyle\forall t\leq s-1:\quad 0\leq\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}\lesssim\frac{1}{k^{2}}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)},

(149)

and thus

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{(s)}\lesssim\frac{1}{k^{2}}a_{q,\times}^{(s)}\lesssim\frac{\ln k}{k^{2}}.

(150)

Similarly, by comparing (

) and (

), we have

\displaystyle\forall t\leq s-1:\quad|\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}|\lesssim\frac{1}{k}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)},

(151)

and thus by (

) we have

\displaystyle|\delta a_{p,2}^{(s)}|\lesssim\left|\frac{\delta a_{p,1}^{(s)}-\delta a_{p,3}^{(s)}}{k-1}\right|\lesssim\frac{1}{k^{2}}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s)},

which yields

\displaystyle|a_{p,2}^{(s)}|\lesssim\frac{\ln k}{k^{2}}.

(152)

(
152
) and (
150
) together show that (
62a
) holds at step
s
.
Similarly, by comparing the gradient expressions of
a_{p,0}^{(t)}
in (

) and

in (

) with (

) and by our choice of

, we immediately know that (

) holds at step
s
.

By (

), we have

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\pi_{1,1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,1}^{(s-1)}+\frac{1}{1+\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(s-1)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle\leq\nu^{(s-1)}_{1}\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,1}^{(s-1)}+\frac{1}{k^{c}}\frac{1}{1+\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(s-1)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}.

(153)

We introduce the following lemma which reveals the ralationships of values at different states.

Lemma 6
.

Under Induction Hypothesis I, when
l=1
,
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
, then for any
t\leq s-1
we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad\frac{V_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}}{V_{1,1}^{(t)}}\asymp 1.

(154)

Moreover, if we also have
\pi_{1,2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\ln k}
, then

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad\frac{V_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}}{V_{1,1}^{(t)}}=1+o(1).

(155)

The proof of Lemma
6
is postponed to Appendix
A.3.4
. By (
148
), (
A.3.2
), Lemma
6
and (

) we immediately know that

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\lesssim\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}.

(156)

This suggests (
61h
) holds at step
s
.
By Lemma
6
and (
A.3.2
) we also know that when
\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
, we have

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\leq(1+o(1))\nu^{(s-1)}_{1}\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,3}^{(s-1)}+\frac{1}{k^{c}}\frac{1}{1+\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(s-1)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})V_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}.

(157)

If
\nu^{(s-1)}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{4}
, by (

), we have
a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\lesssim 1
, and by (

) and (

) in Induction Hypothesis I we have
a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\leq C
for some constant

. And from the first two relations in (

\xi_{2}^{(s-1)}\lesssim 1
, and thus by (

) we have

\displaystyle\text{When }\nu^{(s-1)}_{1}\geq\frac{1}{4}:\quad\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\gtrsim\frac{1}{k}.

(158)

If
\nu^{(s-1)}_{1}\leq\frac{1}{4}
,
\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{k}}
, by comparing (

) and (

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\leq\frac{2}{3}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}.

(159)

When

,

, by (

), Lemma

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\leq\frac{1}{k^{c_{2}}}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\leq\frac{2}{3}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}.

(160)

The above two inequalities suggest (
62d
) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step
s
.

On the other hand, for any
t\leq s-1
, we have

\displaystyle-\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_a_q_0_simplified_balanced_phase_1}}{\leq}\frac{1}{1+e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t)}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)V_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:delta_a_q_x_T_11_bound_2}}{\leq}(1+o(1))\frac{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}{1+e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t)}}}\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}.

(161)

This indicates that

\displaystyle-\left(a_{q,0}^{(t)}-a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}\right)\lesssim\frac{1}{e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(t)}-a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}\right).

(162)

By (
62c
) and the above inequality, we know that (
61k
) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step
s
.

By (
62d
), (
134
) and (
141
), we have for any
s-1\leq T_{1,1}
:

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k-1]:\quad\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\gtrsim\frac{1}{k}.

(163)

By (
133c
) and Induction Hypothesis I, we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s)}(\texttt{down})=(1+o(1))\frac{1-\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(s)}(\texttt{up})}{k}\overset{\eqref{eq:pi_1_2h-1_up=o(1)}}{=}\frac{1+o(1)}{k},

and thus

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k]:\quad V_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}\geq\pi_{1,2h-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down})=(1+o(1))\frac{1}{k}.

Plugging (

This combined with (

By (

) and (

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1-e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}}{1+e^{-2a_{c,1}^{(s-1)}}}\left(1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\right)\bigg)V_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}>0.

Note that by (

, (

) and (

\delta a_{b,0,A}^{(s-1)}
as

\displaystyle\forall t\in[T_{1,1}+1,s-1]:\quad|\delta a_{b,0,A}^{(t)}|\lesssim\frac{\ln k}{N}\left(\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t)}+\delta a_{q,0}^{(t)}\right),

and thus by (

) or (

) we have

\displaystyle|a_{b,0,A}^{(s)}-a_{b,0,A}^{(T_{1,1})}|\lesssim\frac{\ln^{2}k}{N}.

And analogous to how we bound

in (

), here we can bound

as

\displaystyle-\frac{\ln k}{Nk}\lesssim a_{b,0,B}^{(s)}-a_{b,0,B}^{(T_{1,1})}\lesssim\frac{k^{3}\ln^{2}k}{N}.

The above two expressions together with (

) and that

) holds at step

imply that (

) holds at step
s
. Combining (

) and (

) hold at step

) holds at step
s
.

Now we show (

) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step

. Note that it implies (

) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step
s
for any

. We consider two sub-cases: (i)

(i)

.

), (

) and (

(215)

) holds at step

when

.

(ii)

a_{p,1}^{(s-1)}\geq 5\ln k
.
We first use contradiction to show that

\displaystyle\text{If }a_{p,1}^{(s-1)}\geq 5\ln k:\quad a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\geq 4\ln k.

) we know that for any

)),

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,1}^{(t^{\prime})}}\gtrsim\ln^{2}k.

A.3.2

\displaystyle\text{If }a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}<3\ln k:\quad\delta a_{q,0}^{(t^{\prime})}+\delta a_{q,\times}^{(t^{\prime})}\geq\frac{1+o(1)}{k^{2}}\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}{\mathbb{P}}^{(t^{\prime})}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{1,2h}\right)\pi_{1,2h}^{(t^{\prime})}(\texttt{up})V_{1,2h+1}^{(t^{\prime})}.

(

which gives

Plugging (

), (

) into (

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,1}^{(s-1)}\gtrsim\frac{1}{k^{5}}.

When

(i)

To show the second relation in (

\displaystyle-a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}<\frac{\ln k}{3}

) holds.

From our choice of

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\leq(3/2+o(1))\ln k.

(ii)

1-\pi_{1,2}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{1}{k^{C^{\prime}}}
(

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k-1]:\quad\kappa_{h}^{(t)}\coloneqq\frac{\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})}{1-\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})}

), (

\displaystyle\kappa_{h}^{(s-1)}=(1+o(1))\frac{\exp\left(\zeta_{h}^{(s-1)}\right)}{\left(h\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(s-1)}}{2h+1}\right)+k-h\right)\left(h\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(s-1)}}{2h}\right)+k-h\right)},

(259)

where

\displaystyle\zeta_{h}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left(1+\frac{\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}{\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}+1}\right)a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}-\frac{1}{1+\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}-\frac{4h+1}{4h+2}a_{p,2}^{(s-1)}

\displaystyle=a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}-a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}+\frac{\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}{1+\frac{h}{h+1}e^{-2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}}\left(a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\right)-\frac{4h+1}{4h+2}a_{p,2}^{(s-1)}.

(260)

Below we first show by contradiction that the second relation in (
65c
) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step
s
. Suppose otherwise, i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(s)}-a_{q,0}^{(s)}>2.1\ln k,

(261)

Then by our gradient updates we have

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}-a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}>2.1\ln k-\frac{1}{2}.

This combined with the fact that
a_{q,\times}^{(t-1)}+a_{q,0}^{(t-1)}>0
guaranteed by Induction Hypothesis I and (

) gives

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k-1]:\quad\zeta_{h}^{(s-1)}>2.1\ln k-2,

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k-1]:\quad\kappa_{h}^{(s-1)}\overset{\eqref{eq:a_p_1_bound_T_11}}{\geq}(1+o(1))\frac{\exp(\zeta_{h}^{(s-1)})}{k^{2}}\gtrsim k^{0.1},

which indicates

\displaystyle\forall h\in[k-1]:\quad\pi_{1,2h}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})=o\left(\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(s-1)}(\texttt{up})\right).

Thus by comparing (

) and (

), and using (

), we know that

\displaystyle\delta a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}-\delta a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}<0,

which suggests

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(s)}-a_{q,0}^{(s)}<a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}-a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\leq 2.1\ln k,

where the last inequality is guaranteed by Induction Hypothesis I. This contradicts (
261
). Thus the second relation in (
65c
) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step
s
.

We now show the first relation in (

) in Induction Hypothesis I holds at step

also by contradiction. Suppose otherwise, i.e.,

\displaystyle a_{q,\times}^{(s)}-a_{q,0}^{(s)}<0,

(

T_{1,1}

Note that by (

\displaystyle e^{2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}\geq e^{2a_{c,0}^{(s-1)}}-e^{2a_{c,0}^{(s^{\prime})}}\gtrsim a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}\left(a_{q,0}^{(s^{\prime})}+a_{q,\times}^{(s^{\prime})}\right)\gtrsim\left(a_{q,\times}^{(s-1)}+a_{q,0}^{(s-1)}\right)\ln k.

(264)

This combined with (
(ii)

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(t)};{\mathcal{P}}_{1})=\underbracket{\frac{1}{k}}_{\text{(a)}}\underbracket{\left(1-\pi_{1,1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)}_{(b)}+

\displaystyle\sum_{h=1}^{k-1}\underbracket{\frac{1}{k}}_{\text{(c)}}\bigg(\underbracket{\prod_{i=1}^{h-1}\pi_{1,2i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\prod_{i=1}^{h}\left(1-(i-1)\pi_{1,2i-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})-\pi_{1,2i-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)}_{(d)}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\underbracket{\pi_{1,2h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-h\pi_{1,2h+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})\right)}_{(e)}\bigg),

\displaystyle\text{(d)}=\underbrace{\prod_{i=1}^{h-1}\pi_{1,2i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})}_{\text{(d1)}}\underbrace{\prod_{i=1}^{h}\left(1-(i-1)\pi_{1,2i-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{down}_{\times})-\pi_{1,2i-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)}_{\text{(d2)}}.

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}\left(1-(i-1)\pi_{1,2i-1}^{(T_{1/2})}(\texttt{down}_{\times})-\pi_{1,2i-1}^{(T_{1/2})}(\texttt{up})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=1}^{k-1}\left(1-\frac{2k^{-1.05}}{k-i+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\prod_{i=2}^{k}\left(1-\frac{2k^{-1.05}}{i}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\prod_{i=2}^{\lfloor k^{0.04}\rfloor}\left(1-2k^{-1.05}\right)\prod_{i=\lfloor k^{0.04}\rfloor+1}^{\lfloor k^{0.08}\rfloor}\left(1-2k^{-1.09}\right)\cdots\prod_{i=\lfloor k^{0.96}\rfloor+1}^{k}\left(1-2k^{-2.01}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-2k^{-1.05}\right)^{k^{0.04}}\left(1-2k^{-1.09}\right)^{k^{0.08}}\cdots\left(1-2k^{-2.01}\right)^{k}.

\displaystyle\geq 1-\frac{c}{k^{1.01}}

for some constant

.

Below we prove (
67d
). All probabilities here are under policy
\pi^{(T_{1/2})}
with a training distribution

) for a fixed

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{\text{goal is not at the first $k-l+1$ leaves $\pi^{(T_{1/2})}$ visits, and }a_{i}\in[k],a_{i}\neq a_{j},\forall i,j\in[l],i\neq j\right\},

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{2}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{a_{l+1}=\texttt{up}\right\},

(306)

and for all

,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{2i-1}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{a_{l+2(i-1)}\in[k]\setminus\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{l},a_{l+2},\cdots,a_{l+2(i-2)}\}\right\},

(307)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{B}}_{2i}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\left\{a_{l+2i-1}=\texttt{up}\right\}.

(308)

Then we have

\displaystyle 1-R\left(\pi^{(T_{1/2})};{\mathcal{P}}_{l}\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1})\Big({\mathbb{P}}(\underbrace{a_{l+1}\neq\texttt{up}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}}_{=1-{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1})})+{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{2}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}){\mathbb{P}}(\{\text{$\pi^{(T_{1/2})}$ fails at some step $h>l+1$}\}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}{\mathcal{B}}_{2})\Big)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1}){\mathbb{P}}(\{\text{$\pi^{(T_{1/2})}$ fails at some step $h>l+1$}\}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}{\mathcal{B}}_{2})

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1})\Big({\mathbb{P}}(a_{l+2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{l+2,\times}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}{\mathcal{B}}_{2})+{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{3}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}){\mathbb{P}}\Big(\{\text{$\pi^{(T_{1/2})}$ fails at some step $h>l+2$}\}|\bigcap_{j=1}^{3}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1}){\mathbb{P}}(a_{l+2}\notin\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{l-1},\texttt{up}\}|{\mathcal{B}}_{1}{\mathcal{B}}_{2}){\mathbb{P}}\Big(\{\text{$\pi^{(T_{1/2})}$ fails at some step $h>l+2$}\}|\bigcap_{j=1}^{3}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big)

\displaystyle\geq\cdots

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1})\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k-l}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{l+2i}\notin\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{l-1},\texttt{up}\}|\bigcap_{j=1}^{2i}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt\cdot\left({\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{2k-l+1}\neq\texttt{up}\Big|\bigcap_{j=1}^{2(k-l)+1}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big)+{\mathbb{P}}\Big({\mathcal{B}}_{2(k-l+1)}\Big|\bigcap_{j=1}^{2(k-l)+1}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big){\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{2k-l+2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{k-l+1,\times}\Big|\bigcap_{j=1}^{2(k-l+1)}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big)\right)

\displaystyle\geq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1})\left(\prod_{i=1}^{k-l}{\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{l+2i}\notin\{a_{1},a_{2},\cdots,a_{l-1},\texttt{up}\}\Big|\bigcap_{j=1}^{2i}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big)\right){\mathbb{P}}\Big(a_{2k-l+2}\in{\mathcal{A}}_{2k-l+2,\times}\Big|\bigcap_{j=1}^{2(k-l)+1}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}\Big).

(309)

Note that conditioned on
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, by Induction Hypothesis I and (
1
), (
1
), (
57
), (
58
) in Lemma
1
, we have

\displaystyle\forall h\in[l]:\quad\varphi_{h}(m)

\displaystyle=-N_{h}(m)\left(a_{p,1}^{(T_{1/2})}+a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}\right)+(h-1)a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}+o(1),

\displaystyle\xi_{h}=-a_{q,0}^{(T_{1/2})},

(310)

and thus conditioned on
{\mathcal{B}}_{1}
, for all
h\in[l]
, we have for all
m\in[k]
:

\displaystyle\pi_{h}^{(T_{1/2})}(m)=(1+o(1))\frac{\exp\left(-N_{h}(m)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{1/2})}+a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}}{h}\right)}{\exp\left(-a_{q,0}^{(T_{1/2})}-\frac{h-1}{h}a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}\right)+(h-1)\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{1/2})}+a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}}{h}\right)+k-h+1}.

(311)

From this, Induction Hypothesis I, and the fact that

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\text{goal is not at the first $k-l+1$ leaves $\pi^{(T_{1/2})}$ visits}\right)=1-\frac{k-l+1}{k^{l}},

(312)

we deduce

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{B}}_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{k-l+1}{k^{l}}\right)\prod_{h=1}^{l}\left(1-(1+o(1))\frac{\exp\left(-a_{q,0}^{(T_{1/2})}-\frac{h-1}{h}a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}\right)+(h-1)\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{1/2})}+a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}}{h}\right)}{\exp\left(-a_{q,0}^{(T_{1/2})}-\frac{h-1}{h}a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}\right)+(h-1)\exp\left(-\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{1/2})}+a_{p,2}^{(T_{1/2})}}{h}\right)+k-h+1}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\left(1-\frac{k-l+1}{k^{l}}\right)\left(1-(1+o(1))\frac{\exp\left(-a_{q,0}^{(T_{1/2})}\right)}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:a_q_0>=1.9lnk_T_12}}{\geq}\left(1-\frac{c_{1}}{k^{2.9}}\right)\left(1-\frac{k-l+1}{k^{l}}\right)

(313)

for some constant
c_{1}>0
.

For all
i\in[k-l+1]
, conditioned on
\bigcap_{j=1}^{2i}{\mathcal{B}}_{j}
, we have when
h=l+2i
:

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{A}}_{l+2i,\times}|=N_{l+2i,\times}=N_{l+2i}(\texttt{up})=i,

(314)

and thus by Induction Hypothesis I and (
1
), (
1
), (

), (

) in Lemma
