For all
i,j\in[k]
, define events

\displaystyle{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{\text{the agent traverses all children of $n_{h_{m}}$ for all $m\in[i-1]$ (if $i>1$),}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{then visits $j$ children of $n_{h_{i}}$ and reach the goal after taking $a_{h_{i}+2(j-1)}$, ${\mathcal{T}}\sim P_{2}$}\},

(617)

where
h_{i}
is defined in (
593
).
And let
[{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}]_{\leq h}
be the "mask of
{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}
up to step
h
", i.e.,

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}]_{\leq h}\coloneqq\left\{\omega\in\Omega:\exists\omega^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j},\text{ such that }s_{h}(\omega^{\prime})\leq s_{h}(\omega)\right\},

(618)

where
\Omega
is the sample space of one full episode. And let

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{h}\coloneqq\pi_{h}|[{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}]_{\leq h}.

(619)

Then we have

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(t)};{\mathcal{P}}_{2})

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi^{(t)}}\left({\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}(1-\widehat{\pi}_{1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}))\Bigg[\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i-1}\bigg(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{k}\Big(1-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2(j^{\prime}-1)}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2(j^{\prime}-1),\times}}\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2(j^{\prime}-1)}^{(t)}(m)\Big)\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i}+2j^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\bigg)

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\cdot\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big(1-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k+1,\times}}\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k+1}^{(t)}(m)\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\cdot\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j}\left(1-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i}+2(j^{\prime}-1)}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-\sum_{m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i}+2(j^{\prime}-1),\times}}\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i}+2(j^{\prime}-1)}^{(t)}(m)\right)\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j-1}\pi_{h_{i}+2j^{\prime}-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)}}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\Bigg[\prod_{i^{\prime}=1}^{i-1}\bigg(\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{k-1}\Big(1-\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)}-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2j^{\prime}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big)\bigg)\cdot\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big(1-\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)}-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\hskip 227.62204pt\cdot\prod_{j^{\prime}=1}^{j-1}\left(1-\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)}-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i}+2j^{\prime}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right).

(620)

where the last line follows from (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
) in the proof of Lemma
8
and our assumption that
\epsilon
is small enough. And from (
A.3.8
), (
653
) we know that there exists some constant
c>0
such that for all
i^{\prime}\in[i-1]
,
j^{\prime}\in[k-1]
:

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2j^{\prime}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{(k-j^{\prime})\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)+\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)},

(621)

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k+1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{(k-i^{\prime})\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)+\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)},

(622)

for all
j^{\prime}\in[j-1]
(if
j>1
):

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i}+2j^{\prime}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{(k-j^{\prime})\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)+\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)},

(623)

for all
i^{\prime}\in[i-1]
:

\displaystyle 1-\widehat{\pi}_{h_{i^{\prime}}+2k}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{k}{k+\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}+\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}\right)}.

(624)

From (
A.3.7
), (
621
), (
622
), (
623
), (
624
) and our choice of
\delta_{2}
we know that for all
t\in[T_{2,1},T_{2}-1]
, the following must hold:

\displaystyle\max\left\{\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)+\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\right)},\frac{k}{k+\exp\left(-\left(1-\frac{c}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}+\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}\right)}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}}

(625)

or otherwise
R(\pi^{(t)};{\mathcal{P}}_{2})>1-\delta_{2}
for some
t\in[T_{2,1},T_{2}-1]
, which contradicts our training setting. From (
81a
) and (
A.3.7
) we deduce that there eixsts constants
c_{3},c_{4}>0
such that

\displaystyle\frac{a_{p,3}^{(T_{2})}}{k}-\left(1-\frac{c_{3}}{\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{q,0}\leq\ln\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}\right),\quad\frac{a_{p,1}^{(T_{2})}}{k}\leq 2\ln\left(\frac{k^{L}}{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}\right)+\frac{c_{4}}{\ln^{2}k}a_{q,0}.

(626)

This gives (
81d
) and (
81e
). From (
81e
), (
78c
) and Lemma
3
we immediately deduce that (
78e
) holds at step
s
for any
s\in[T_{1}+1,T_{2}]
.

Moreover, from (
A.3.7
), (
616
) and (
592
) we also know that for all
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}-1]
:

\displaystyle 1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\geq\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}}.

(627)

And from Lemma
3
and Induction Hypothesis III we also know that

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\geq\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)}.

(628)

By (
628
), (
627
), (
596a
) and the fact that (c.f. (
691
))

Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\geq\frac{1+o(1)}{k},\,\,\forall i\in[k-1]

guaranteed by our policy computation in the proof of Lemma
8
,
we have

\displaystyle\forall t\in[T_{1},T_{2}-1]:\quad\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}\geq\min\left\{\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)},\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}}\right\}\geq\frac{\epsilon^{1.1}}{k^{L}}.

(629)

(
629
) implies (
78c
) holds.
(
629
) combined with (
81e
) and Lemma
3
also yields (
82
).

Finally, we show (
83
) holds for any full
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
with depth
l\in[L]
(that may not be a perfect
k
-ary tree). First note that by a similar policy computation as in the proof of Lemma
8
we know that given any
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
with depth
l\in[L]
, (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
) still hold, i.e.,

\displaystyle\text{When }c_{h}=0\text{ and }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0:\quad\pi_{h}^{(T_{2})}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)},

(630)

\displaystyle\text{when }c_{h}=\times:\quad 1-\pi_{h}^{(T_{2})}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)},

(631)

\displaystyle\text{when }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]:\quad\pi_{h}^{(T_{2})}(j)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1.05+o(1)},\,\,\forall j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}.

(632)

And similar as how we compute (
A.3.8
), (
653
) in the proof of Lemma
8
, by (
81b
), (
81c
) and our choice of
\delta_{2}
we can compute that, for any
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
with depth
l\in[L]
, we have

\displaystyle\text{When }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]:\quad\pi_{h}^{(T_{2})}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}},

\displaystyle\text{When }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k:\quad 1-\pi_{h}^{(T_{2})}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}}.

(633)

Similar as how we define
{\mathcal{C}}_{2,i}^{j}
in (
A.3.7
) and use it to lower bound
R(\pi^{(t)};{\mathcal{P}}_{2})
in (
A.3.7
), here we define the event

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g)\coloneqq\{\text{the agent follows the DFS rule on ${\mathcal{T}}$ until it first reaches }g\},

(634)

and let
{\mathcal{V}}_{\text{leaf}}({\mathcal{T}})
denote the set of leaves of
{\mathcal{T}}
.
For any fixed goal node
g_{\star}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{leaf}}({\mathcal{T}})
,
on the event
{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})
, the agent reaches the target goal node
g_{\star}
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(T_{2})};({\mathcal{T}},g_{\star}))\geq{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi^{(T_{2})}}\left({\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})\right).

(635)

For each step
h
, let
{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(g_{\star})
denote the event that the trajectory has
followed the DFS rule toward
g_{\star}
up to step
h
. Equivalently,

{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(g_{\star})
is the prefix event induced by
{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})
up to step
h
.
Let

\displaystyle\widehat{\pi}_{h}^{(T_{2})}\coloneqq\pi_{h}^{(T_{2})}\mid{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(g_{\star}).

(636)

Conditioned on
{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(g_{\star})
, the one-step probability of deviating from the
DFS rule is

\displaystyle\eta_{h}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})\coloneqq\begin{cases}\widehat{\pi}_{h}^{(T_{2})}(\texttt{up})+\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}\widehat{\pi}_{h}^{(T_{2})}(i),&\text{if }c_{h}=0,\ |{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\leq k-1,\\
1-\widehat{\pi}_{h}^{(T_{2})}(\texttt{up}),&\text{if }c_{h}=\times\text{ or }(c_{h}=0\text{ and }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k).\end{cases}

(637)

By (
630
) and (
A.3.7
), for every
h
,

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\eta_{h}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})]\leq\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}}.

(638)

Since a deviation from
{\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})
must occur at some step
h\leq H
,
we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi^{(T_{2})}}\left({\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})^{c}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\{{\mathcal{F}}_{h}(g_{\star})\}\eta_{h}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{h=1}^{H}{\mathbb{E}}[\eta_{h}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})]\leq H\cdot\frac{\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}}{k^{L}}\leq\epsilon^{1.02+o(1)}\leq\epsilon,

(639)

where we used
H\lesssim k^{L}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle R(\pi^{(T_{2})};({\mathcal{T}},g_{\star}))\overset{\eqref{eq:R_pi_T_2_lb_fixed_goal_phase2}}{\geq}{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi^{(T_{2})}}\left({\mathcal{E}}({\mathcal{T}};g_{\star})\right)\geq 1-\epsilon.

(640)

Since
g_{\star}\in{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathrm{leaf}}({\mathcal{T}})
was arbitrary,
(
83
) follows.

A.3.8
Proof of Lemma
8

Policy computation.

To compute the gradients, we first compute the policies on trees with depth 2.
Note that under our setting,
on any perfect
k
-ary tree of depth
l
,
c_{h}=0
if
h\leq l
, and if
c_{h}=\times
at some step
h
, then
c_{h-1}=0
, and
c_{h+1}=0
if the environment doesn’t terminate at step
h
.
Therefore, at any step
h\in[H]
, we have

\displaystyle N_{h,\times}\leq\begin{cases}\max\{0,\frac{h-l}{2}\},&\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
\max\{0,\frac{h-l+1}{2}\},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times.\end{cases}

(641)

In addition, the level the agent is at a tree of depth
l
(root is level 0, and leaves are level
l
) at step
h
equals
\sum_{i=1}^{k}N_{h}(i)-N_{h}(\texttt{up})
. As a result, we have

\displaystyle 0\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}N_{h}(i)-N_{h}(\texttt{up})\leq l,\quad\sum_{i=1}^{k}N_{h}(i)+N_{h}(\texttt{up})=h-1.

(642)

The above two relations yield

\displaystyle\frac{h-1}{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{k}N_{h}(i)\leq\frac{h-1+l}{2},\quad\max\left\{0,\frac{h-1-l}{2}\right\}\leq N_{h}(\texttt{up})\leq\frac{h-1}{2}.

(643)

By (
641
), (
57
) and Lemma
3
, we have

\displaystyle\xi_{h}=\begin{cases}-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0},&\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
a_{q,\times}+O\left(\frac{k^{2}}{\ln(1/\epsilon)}\right),&\text{if }c_{h}=\times.\end{cases}

(644)

Let
\widetilde{\phi}_{h}\coloneqq(\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(1),\ldots,\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(k),\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(\texttt{up}))^{\top}
with

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(i)\coloneqq\frac{\varphi_{h}(i)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+(h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)e^{a_{b,2}}},\,\,\forall i\in[k],\quad\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\xi_{h}.

(645)

Then by Lemma
1
we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}=\mathsf{softmax}\left(\widetilde{\phi}_{h}\right).

(646)

In addition, when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
, for all
i\in[k]
, by Lemma
1
, Lemma
3
, Induction Hypothesis III and (
643
) we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}+\frac{1}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}\left(N_{h}(\texttt{up})\frac{a_{p,\texttt{up}}^{(t)}}{k}+\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}+(h-1-N_{h}(\texttt{up}))a_{p,2}^{(t)}-N_{h}(i)\left(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+o(1)}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}+O\left(\frac{k}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)a_{p,1}^{(t)}.

(647)

By (
646
), (
A.3.8
), (
644
), (
76c
) we have

\displaystyle\text{When }c_{h}=0\text{ and }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0:\quad\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k}\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}+\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1.05+O\left(1/\ln^{2}k\right)}.

(648)

Note
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
, as in (
44
) we define
{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}=\emptyset
if
c_{h}=\times
. Then by (
646
), (
A.3.8
), (
644
) and (
76d
) we have

\displaystyle\text{When }c_{h}=\times:\,\,1-\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=k\exp\left(-\left(1+\frac{1}{k}+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+o(1)}}\right)\right)a_{q,\times}-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1.05+O\left(1/k\right)}.

(649)

Similarly, when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1
, by Lemma
1
, Lemma
3
and Induction Hypothesis III, we have

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=a_{p,2}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)})

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}-a_{b,0}^{(t)}}}\left(-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}-a_{p,2}^{(t)}+\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)})\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+o(1)}}\right)

\displaystyle=a_{p,2}^{(t)}-\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}(a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)})+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)a_{p,1}^{(t)}.

(650)

Especially, when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k
, by (
A.3.8
) and (
52a
) we have

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k]:\quad\widetilde{\phi}_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}+\frac{1}{e^{a_{b,1}^{(t)}-a_{b,0}^{(t)}}}\left(-\frac{a_{p,0}^{(t)}}{k^{2}}+\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}\right)+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+o(1)}}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}+O\left(\frac{1}{k\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)a_{p,1}^{(t)}.

(651)

Then by (
646
), (
644
), (
52a
) and the above two expressions we have when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
:

\displaystyle\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{(k-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)+\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:a_p_3_bound_phase2}}{\leq}\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{(k-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|)\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}}{2(k-1)}-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)+\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}\overset{\eqref{eq:a_p_1_2_bound_T_1}}{}

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\frac{1.05}{2}+o(1)},

(652)

and

\displaystyle\text{When }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k:\quad\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}+\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}\right)}{k+\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}+\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}\right)}.

(653)

And when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
:

\displaystyle\forall j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}:\quad\pi_{h}(j)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right),

(654)

which, combined with (
76b
) and Induction Hypothesis III, further indicates for all
j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}
:

\displaystyle\text{When }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k-1:\quad\pi_{h}(j)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{1.05+O\left(1/k\right)},

\displaystyle\text{when }|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-2]:\quad\pi_{h}(j)

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon^{\frac{C}{k}}\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)

(655)

for some constant
C>0
.
When
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k
, from (
A.3.8
) we can see that

\displaystyle\forall j\in[k]:\quad\pi_{h}(j)

\displaystyle=(1+o(1/k^{2}))\frac{1-\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})}{k}\overset{\eqref{eq:pi_h_up_Ax=k_phase2}}{=}\frac{1}{k+\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}+\frac{a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k}\right)}.

(656)

Gradient computation.

For all
i\in[k]
, we define events

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\coloneqq\{\text{the environment has not terminated at step }h_{i},l=2\},

(657)

where
h_{i}
is defined in (
593
). We also define
h_{k+1}=+\infty
.
Let

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k-1]:\quad\pi_{2,i}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq\pi_{h_{i}+2k}(\texttt{up})|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i},\quad Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq Q_{h_{i}+2k}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i},

\displaystyle\pi_{2,k}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq 0,\quad Q_{2,k}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq 0.

(658)

Note that
\pi_{2,i}(\texttt{up})
and
Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})
are well-defined because they are invariant to the order of children of
n_{h_{j}}
(
j\in[i]
) and the root visited under the balanced distribution.
We also define the following events for all
i^{\prime},i\in[k-1]
and
j\in\{0,1,\ldots,k-1\}
:

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{\text{the agent traverses all children of $n_{h_{m}}$ for all $m\in[i-1]$ (if $i>1$),}

\displaystyle\qquad\text{then visits $j$ children of $n_{h_{i}}$ and arrives at $n_{h_{i}}$ at step $h_{i}+2j$, $l=2$}\},

\displaystyle\forall i^{\prime}<i:\quad\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i^{\prime},j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{\text{the agent traverses all children of $n_{h_{m}}$ for all $m\in[i]\setminus\{i^{\prime}\}$},

\displaystyle\qquad\text{visits $j$ children of $n_{h_{i^{\prime}}}$, and arrives at $n_{h_{i}}$ at step $h_{i}+2k$, $l=2$}\},

(659)

and
\forall i,j\in[k]
:

\displaystyle\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{\times,j}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\{\text{the agent traverses all children of $n_{h_{m}}$ for all $m\in[i-1]$ (if $i>1$),}

\displaystyle\text{then visits $j$ children of $n_{h_{i}}$ and arrives at the $j$-th child of $n_{h_{i}}$ visited at step $h_{i}+2j-1$, $l=2$}\}.

(660)

For any sequence of random variables
\{x_{h}\}_{h\in[H]}
and policy
\pi
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\pi}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}x_{h}\Big|l=2\right]={\mathbb{E}}_{\pi}\left[x_{1}|l=2\right]+\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right){\mathbb{E}}_{\pi}\left[\sum_{h=h_{i}}^{h_{i+1}-1}x_{h}\Big|l=2,{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right].

(661)

And for any
\pi=\pi^{(t)}
(
t\in[T_{1},s-1]
), we have by our policy computation (c.f. (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
), (
653
), (
A.3.8
)) we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,1}\right)

\displaystyle=1-\pi_{1}(\texttt{up})\overset{\eqref{eq:pi_h_up_Ax=0_c=0_phase2}}{\geq}1-\epsilon^{1.05+O\left(1/\ln^{2}k\right)},

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{2,\cdots,k\}:\quad{\mathbb{P}}^{\pi}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)

\displaystyle=(1+o(\epsilon^{0.5}))\frac{k-i+1}{k}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}\pi_{2,j}(\texttt{up}).

(662)

With the above preparations, we are ready to simplify the gradients in Lemma
1
.
All expectations here are conditioned on tree depth
l=2
and policy

unless otherwise specified, i.e.,

We first compute

. From (

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1/k^{2})}{k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1\}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+(1+o(1/k^{2})){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0\}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\frac{N_{h}(j)}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\Big|j\in[k]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+(1+o(1/k^{2})){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)\frac{N_{h}(j)}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big|j\in[k]\right].

(663)

Applying (

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1\}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right]

\displaystyle=(1+o(1/k^{2}))\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{(k-i+1)k-j}{(k-i+1)k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\pi_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}\right)\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,j}\right].

), we obtain

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,3}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{k-i}{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i}\Bigg((1+o(1))\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{(k-i)\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}+\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}\Bigg)Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}).

(701)

This gives (
596b
).

Now we bound
\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}
.
By (
52c
) and Lemma
3
we have

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=(1+o(1/k^{2})){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1,j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)G_{h}^{(t)}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt+\left(1+o(1/k^{2})\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\frac{N_{h}(j)}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big|j\in[k]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\left(1+o(1/k^{2})\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0\}\frac{N_{h}(j)}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(j)-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\Big|j\in[k]\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 9.24994pt-\left(1+o(1/k^{2})\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1,j\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{N_{h}(j)}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}-a_{b,2}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(j)-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right].

(702)

Similar as in (
A.3.8
), for the first term we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1,j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)G_{h}^{(t)}\right]

\displaystyle=(1+o(1/k^{2}))\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{(k-i+1)k-j}{(k-i+1)k}\cdot\frac{1}{j}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\mathbbm{1}\{m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i}+2j,\times}\}\pi_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}(m)G_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,j}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1/k^{2})}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{(k-i+1)k-j}{(k-i+1)k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\pi_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}(m)G_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,j},m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i}+2j,\times}\right].

(703)

By (
A.3.8
), (
667
) we have for all
i\in[k]
(recall
\epsilon
is small enough,
Q_{2,k}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})=0
):

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k-1}\frac{(k-i+1)k-j}{(k-i+1)k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\pi_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}(m)G_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,j},m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i}+2j,\times}\right]

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\frac{(k-i+1)k-k+1}{(k-i+1)k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\pi_{h_{i}+2(k-1)}^{(t)}(m)G_{h_{i}+2(k-1)}^{(t)}\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,k-1},m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i}+2(k-1),\times}\right]

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)\left(\frac{1}{(k-i+1)k}+\frac{k-i}{k-i+1}\pi_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right).

(704)

And by (
656
) we have for all
i\in[k]
:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\pi_{h_{i}+2k}^{(t)}(m)G_{h_{i}+2k}^{(t)}\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i},m\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h_{i}+2k,\times}\right]

\displaystyle=(1+o(1/k^{2}))\frac{\pi_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)}{k}Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}).

(705)

Therefore, by (
A.3.8
), (
A.3.8
), (
705
), (
674
), we can rewrite the first term in (
A.3.8
) as follows:

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1,j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(j)G_{h}^{(t)}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{k}\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\Bigg[\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\frac{1}{(k-i+1)k}+\frac{k-i}{k-i+1}\pi_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)+\frac{k-i}{k-i+1}\frac{\pi_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)}{k}Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:PP_gE_2_i_simplified_balanced}}{=}\frac{1+o(1)}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i-1}\Bigg[\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\cdot\left(\frac{1}{k}+(k-i)\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)+\frac{k-i}{k}\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Bigg].

(706)

Similar as how we bound the last two terms in (
A.3.8
), By Induction Hypothesis III and Lemma
3
we can compute that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i-1}\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\left(\ln(1/\epsilon)\right)^{1+O(1/N)}}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}+a_{p,2}^{(t)}}{k-1}\right)\left(\frac{1}{k}+(k-i)\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)

\displaystyle=o\Bigg(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{k-i}{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i}\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Bigg),

(707)

and the last three terms in (
A.3.8
) can also be bounded by

\displaystyle o\Bigg(\frac{1}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{k-i}{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i}\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Bigg).

(708)

By the above two relations we have

\displaystyle\delta a_{p,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+o(1)}{k^{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{k-i}{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i}\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}).

(709)

This gives (
596a
).

Now we compute
\delta a_{p,0}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\quad+(1+o(1/k^{2})){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right]

\displaystyle\quad+(1+o(1/k^{2})){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1\}\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}-a_{b,0}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right].

(710)

Applying (

) to the first term of (

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:PP_gE_2_i_simplified_balanced}}{=}-\pi_{1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})G_{1}^{(t)}+(1+o(1))\sum_{i=1}^{k}\frac{k-i+1}{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i-1}\left(\pi_{h_{i}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[Q_{h_{i}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h_{i}}^{(t)}\Big|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right].

(711)

Same as how we compute (

), we can compute that

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\geq\exp\left(c_{3}\frac{a_{q,\times}}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\pi_{1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

for some constant

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c_{4}\frac{\ln(1/\epsilon)}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{2,1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

(713)

for some constant

Applying (

A.3.8

, Induction Hypothesis III, we have (recall we define

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{(k-i+1)k-j}{(k-i+1)k}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\pi_{h_{i}+2j-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{h_{i}+2j-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{h_{i}+2j-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{\times,j}\right]

\displaystyle=(1+o(1))\sum_{i=1}^{k}{\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{(k-i+1)k-j}{(k-i+1)k}\left(1-\pi_{h_{i}+2j-1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{\times,j}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[G_{h_{i}+2j}^{(t)}\Big|\overline{{\mathcal{E}}}_{2,i}^{i,j}\right]

where the last line follows from (

). Then similar as how we compute the last term of

(715)

for some constant

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{ke^{a_{b,1}-a_{b,0}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k-1}\frac{k-i}{k}\left(\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)^{i}\Bigg((1+o(1))\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{\ln k}{(k-i)\exp\left(\frac{a_{p,1}^{(t)}-a_{p,3}^{(t)}}{k-1}+\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}\Bigg)Q_{2,i}^{(t)}(\texttt{up}).

(716)

\displaystyle\quad-(1+o(1/k^{2})){\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1\}\frac{N_{h}(\texttt{up})}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}-a_{b,2}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right].

Notice that for all

\displaystyle N_{h_{i}}(\texttt{up})|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}=(i-1)(k+1),\,\,N_{h_{i}+2j-1}(\texttt{up})|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}^{\times,j}=(i-1)(k+1)+j-1,

\displaystyle N_{h_{i}+2j}(\texttt{up})|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}^{i,j}=(i-1)(k+1)+j.

Thus same as how we rewrite the first term of (

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}\sum_{i=1}^{k}(i-1)(k+1){\mathbb{P}}\left({\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right)\left(\pi_{h_{i}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right){\mathbb{E}}\left[Q_{h_{i}}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h_{i}}^{(t)}\Big|{\mathcal{E}}_{2,i}\right]

And same as in (

), we can bound the above term as

\displaystyle\left|{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=\times\}\frac{N_{h}(\texttt{up})}{e^{a_{b,0}-a_{b,2}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right]\right|

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-c_{7}\frac{\ln(1/\epsilon)}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(1-\overline{\pi}_{2}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\right)Q_{2,1}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})

for some constant

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\left[\sum_{h=1}^{H}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\geq 1\}\frac{N_{h}(\texttt{up})}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}-a_{b,2}}}\pi_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})\left(Q_{h}^{(t)}(\texttt{up})-G_{h}^{(t)}\right)\right]

Appendix B
Proof of Proposition
1
and Proposition
2

B.1
Proof of Proposition
1

For a fixed tree
{\mathcal{T}}
and policy
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
, let
H^{\pi}({\mathcal{T}})
denote the expected number of steps to hit the goal when the goal is generated by the balanced latent process
p_{1}^{a}=\cdots=p_{k}^{a}=\frac{1}{k}
. Define

\displaystyle\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{T}}\sim p_{\text{env,tree}}^{l}}\left[H^{\pi}({\mathcal{T}})\right].

(726)

We will show that
\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)
is the same for all
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
and
l\in[L]
.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,r}
denote the collection of all posterior laws of the current unresolved subtree that can arise as follows: first sample an original tree from

p_{\textnormal{env,tree}}^{l}
, then condition on a reachable state and on the event that the goal lies in the current unresolved subtree rooted at the node the agent is at, with remaining depth at most
d
, and with exactly
r
unresolved children at the current node.
For
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,r}

and a continuation policy
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
, let
H_{q}^{\pi}
denote the expected remaining number of steps to hit the goal.

We claim that for every
d\geq 1
, every
r\in[k]
, and every
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,r}
, the quantity
H_{q}^{\pi}

is independent of
\pi
for all
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
. We prove this by lexicographical induction on
(d,r)
: we regulate
(d^{\prime},r^{\prime})<(d,r)
if either
d^{\prime}<d
or
d^{\prime}=d
and
r^{\prime}<r
.

Base case:
d=1
.

If
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,r}
, then the agent is at a node whose children are all leaves.
Let
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}\subseteq[k]
be the set of currently unresolved children and
r=|{\mathcal{U}}_{q}|
. Since the latent goal process is balanced and the
prior over child positions is permutation-invariant, conditional on the current state, the correct child is uniformly distributed
over
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
. And any policy in
\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
must explore the children in
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
in some order before
terminating. Therefore, the expected remaining number of steps is

H_{q}^{\pi}=\frac{1}{r}\sum_{i=1}^{r}\bigl(2(i-1)+1\bigr)=r,

which is independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
.

Induction step.

Fix
d\geq 2
,
r\in[k]
, and assume the claim holds for every
(d^{\prime},r^{\prime})<(d,r)
. We show it holds for
(d,r)
.
Fix any
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,r}
, and let
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}\subseteq[k]
be the set of currently unresolved children and
r=|{\mathcal{U}}_{q}|
. If
r=1
, there is only one unresolved child, so every policy in
\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
must choose it next. If this child is a leaf, then
H_{q}^{\pi}=1
. Otherwise, after moving into that child, we obtain a new posterior
q^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d-1,k}
. Hence

H_{q}^{\pi}=1+H_{q^{\prime}}^{\pi},

and by induction hypothesis, the right-hand side is independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
. In the following we assume
2\leq r\leq k
.

For
{\mathcal{T}}\sim q
, for each
j\in{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
, write
{\mathcal{T}}_{j}
for the child subtree rooted at the
j
-th child of the current node, and let
h({\mathcal{T}}_{j})
be the number of steps needed to fully explore
{\mathcal{T}}_{j}
and return to its root. If child
j
is chosen next and is incorrect (the child subtree doesn’t contain the goal), let
q_{j}^{\times}
denote the posterior after fully exploring
{\mathcal{T}}_{j}
and returning to the current node, and ruling out child
j
. If child
j
is chosen next and is correct and nonterminal, let
q_{j}^{\checkmark}
denote the posterior after moving down into that child.
For any
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
, since
\pi
must explore all children in
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
before hitting the goal, we can let
a^{\pi}(q)\in{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
denote the first child that
\pi
chooses from the current state (without loss of generality, we assume
\pi
is deterministic; if
\pi
is randomized, it can be handled by conditioning on the realized internal randomness and average afterward). There are two cases: (i) with probability
1/r
, the chosen child is the correct one; (ii) with probability
1-1/r
, it’s not.

If the chosen child is correct, then after paying
1
step to move down into that child, there are two subcases. If
{\mathcal{T}}_{a^{\pi}(q)}
is a leaf, then the goal is reached immediately and no continuation cost is incurred. If
{\mathcal{T}}_{a^{\pi}(q)}
is not a leaf, then we obtain a posterior
q^{\checkmark}_{a^{\pi}(q)}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d-1,k}
.
By induction hypothesis on depth, the continuation value

H_{q^{\checkmark}_{a^{\pi}(q)}}\coloneqq H_{q^{\checkmark}_{a^{\pi}(q)}}^{\pi}

is independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
.
If the chosen child is incorrect, then we pay
2+h({\mathcal{T}}_{a^{\pi}(q)})
steps to fully explore that child and return, after which we obtain a posterior

q^{\times}_{a^{\pi}(q)}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,r-1}
. By induction hypothesis, the continuation value

H_{q^{\times}_{a^{\pi}(q)}}\coloneqq H_{q^{\times}_{a^{\pi}(q)}}^{\pi}

is also independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
. Therefore, the expected remaining number of steps is

\displaystyle H_{q}^{\pi}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{T}}\sim q}\Biggl[\frac{1}{r}\,\mathbf{1}\!\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{a^{\pi}(q)}\text{ is a leaf}\right\}+\frac{1}{r}\,\mathbf{1}\!\left\{{\mathcal{T}}_{a^{\pi}(q)}\text{ is not a leaf}\right\}\left(1+H_{q^{\checkmark}_{a^{\pi}(q)}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 40.00006pt+\frac{r-1}{r}\left(2+h({\mathcal{T}}_{a^{\pi}(q)})+H_{q^{\times}_{a^{\pi}(q)}}\right)\Biggr].

(727)

Since by construction, every
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,r}
is invariant under permutations of the children within
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
, the random vector family
({\mathcal{T}}_{j},q_{j}^{\checkmark},q_{j}^{\times})_{j\in{\mathcal{U}}_{q}}
is exchangeable under
q
, i.e., for any permutation
\sigma
of
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
,

\displaystyle(T_{j},q_{j}^{\checkmark},q_{j}^{\times})_{j\in{\mathcal{U}}_{q}}\overset{d}{=}({\mathcal{T}}_{\sigma(j)},q_{\sigma(j)}^{\checkmark},q_{\sigma(j)}^{\times})_{j\in{\mathcal{U}}_{q}}.

(728)

Therefore the expectation on the right-hand side of (
B.1
) is the same no matter which child in
{\mathcal{U}}_{q}
is chosen first. As a result,
H_{q}^{\pi}
is independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
. This proves the induction step.

Finally, for each
l\in[L]
, let
q_{l}
be the posterior law at the root induced by

p_{\textnormal{env,tree}}^{l}
. Then

\displaystyle\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)=H_{q_{l}}^{\pi}

(729)

is independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
.
And by our definition of the environment distribution
{\mathcal{P}}
in (
27
), we have

\displaystyle\overline{H}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\omega_{l}\,\overline{H}^{\pi}(l).

(730)

Because each
\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)
is independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
, the weighted sum

\overline{H}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})
is also independent of
\pi\in\Pi_{\textnormal{success}}
. This proves the proposition.

B.2
Proof of Proposition
2

Proof of part 1:
\pi^{\star}\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
has the minimum expected trajectory length.

First of all, by definition, it’s obvious that

\pi^{\star}\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
. For any fixed tree
{\mathcal{T}}
and policy
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
, we let
H^{\pi}({\mathcal{T}})
denote the expected number of steps to hit the goal when the goal is generated by the imbalanced goal distribution (
26
). Same as (
726
), for the imbalanced case we also define

\displaystyle\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathcal{T}}\sim p_{\text{env,tree}}^{l}}\left[H^{\pi}({\mathcal{T}})\right].

Then by the definition of the environment distribution
{\mathcal{P}}
in (
27
), we have

\displaystyle H^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})=\sum_{l=1}^{L}\omega_{l}\overline{H}^{\pi}(l).

(731)

Therefore, to show part 1, it’s enough to show that, for every
l\in[L]
,
\pi^{\star}
uniquely minimizes
\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)
among all policies in
\Pi_{\text{success}}
.

Fix
l\in[L]
. For
d\geq 1
and nonempty
{\mathcal{U}}\subseteq[k]
, let
{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}}
be the collection of all posterior laws
q
of the current unresolved subtree that can arise as follows:

1.

sample the original tree from
p_{\text{env,tree}}^{l}
;

2.

sample the goal from the goal process
p^{a}
;

3.

condition on a reachable state
s
and on the event that the goal lies in the unresolved subtree rooted at the node the agent is currently at;

4.

require that the remaining depth of that unresolved subtree is at most
d
, and that the currently unresolved child set is
{\mathcal{U}}
.

For
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}}
and
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
, we let
H^{\pi}_{q}
denote the expected number of steps to hit the goal.

We claim that for every
d\geq 1
, every nonempty
{\mathcal{U}}\subseteq[k]
, and every
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}}
, the unique minimizer of
H^{\pi}_{q}
over
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
is the ranked DFS policy: at the current node, explore the unresolved children in decreasing order of
p^{a}_{j}
for
j\in{\mathcal{U}}
; when the current node has no unresolved child left, takes
up
; after moving to a child, apply the same rule recursively at the new node.

Let
r\coloneqq|{\mathcal{U}}|
.
We prove the above claim by lexicographic induction on the tuple
(d,r)
: we regulate
(d^{\prime},r^{\prime})<(d,r)
if either
d^{\prime}<d
or
d^{\prime}=d
and
r^{\prime}<r
.

Fix any
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}}
, and let

\displaystyle S_{\mathcal{U}}\coloneqq\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{U}}}p^{a}_{j}.

(732)

For each
j\in{\mathcal{U}}
, let
G_{j}
be the event that the goal lies in the
j
-th child subtree of the current node. We’ll use the following two facts:

•

Fact 1: posterior probability of the correct child

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}_{q}(G_{j})=\frac{p^{a}_{j}}{S_{\mathcal{U}}},\,\,\forall j\in{\mathcal{U}}.

This is guaranteed by our goal generation process.

•

Fact 2: Under
q
, the unresolved child subtrees
(T_{j})_{j\in{\mathcal{U}}}
are exchangeable in shape across
j\in{\mathcal{U}}
. This is because the prior
p^{l}_{\text{env,tree}}
is uniform over all full
k
-ary trees of depth
l
.

Base case:
d=1
.

Let
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,{\mathcal{U}}}
and write
r\coloneqq|{\mathcal{U}}|
. Then all unresolved children of the current node are leaves. Any policy
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
must traverse all
r
child subtrees in some order before goal is found (note that taking
up
now permanently exits the current unresolved subtree; under the conditioning defining
q
, this yields failure with probability 1; taking
up
at any node when it still has unexplored children leads to a positive failure probability). Thus any
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
is determined by an order

\sigma=(\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(r)),

of the elements of
{\mathcal{U}}
, where
\sigma(i)\in{\mathcal{U}}
is the child inspected in position
i
. Note it suffices to consider deterministic orders of
{\mathcal{U}}
: a randomized policy
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
induces a probability distribution
\mu
over deterministic orders
\sigma
of
{\mathcal{U}}
. If
H_{q}(\sigma)
denotes the expected remaining path length under order
\sigma
and optimal continuation thereafter, then

\displaystyle H_{q}(\sigma)=\sum_{\sigma}\mu(\sigma)H_{q}(\sigma).

Hence
H_{q}^{\pi}\geq\min_{\sigma}H_{q}(\sigma)
. Moreover, if the minimizing order is unique, then any optimal policy must put probability one on that order.
If the correct child is inspected in position
i
, then the number of steps until success is exactly
2(i-1)+1
. Therefore, the expected number of steps until success is

\displaystyle H_{q}^{\pi}=\sum_{i=1}^{r}{\mathbb{P}}_{q}(G_{\sigma(i)})\left(2(i-1)+1\right)=\frac{1}{S_{\mathcal{U}}}\sum_{i=1}^{r}p^{a}_{\sigma(i)}\left(2(i-1)+1\right),

where the second equality follows from Fact 1. By rearrangement inequality and our assumption that
p_{1}>p_{2}>\cdots>p_{k}
we know that the unique optimal order is

\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(r)\quad\text{given by decreasing order of $p^{a}_{j}$ on ${\mathcal{U}}$}.

This proves the claim for all
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{1,{\mathcal{U}}}
.

Induction step.

Fix
d\geq 2
, nonempty
{\mathcal{U}}\subseteq[k]
, and assume the claim holds for every
(d^{\prime},r^{\prime})<(d,r)
. We prove it for
q\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}}
.

We still let
r\coloneqq|{\mathcal{U}}|
. If
r=1
, i.e.
{\mathcal{U}}=\{j\}
for some
j\in[k]
, then there is only one unresolved child. Any
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
must choose
\texttt{down}_{j}
next. If that child is a leaf, success occurs in one step; if not, after moving down we obtain a posterior
q^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{d-1,[k]}
, and by induction hypothesis, the unique optimal continuation from there is the ranked DFS policy. So the claim holds for
r=1
. Below we consider
2\leq r\leq k
.

Note that under
q
, any
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
must, at the current node, simply choose an order in which to inspect the children in
{\mathcal{U}}
. So let

\sigma=(\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(r))

be any deterministic order of
{\mathcal{U}}
. We define two constants:

•

Let
A_{q}
be the expected remaining steps conditioned on the child currently chosen is the correct child and, after moving into it, one continues optimally from there.

•

Let
B_{q}
be the expected steps one fully explores that child subtree and returns to the current node, conditioned on the child currently chosen is incorrect

By Fact 2, both
A_{q}
and
B_{q}
are independent of the identity of the chosen child
j\in{\mathcal{U}}
. Let
I\in\{1,\cdots,r\}
by the random rank of the correct child in the order
\sigma
. By Fact 1, we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(I=i)={\mathbb{P}}_{q}(G_{\sigma(i)})=\frac{p^{a}_{\sigma(i)}}{S_{\mathcal{U}}},\,\,\forall i\in[r].

Conditional on
I=i
, the first
i-1
children are wrong, each costing
B_{q}
, and the
i
-th child is correct, costing
A_{q}
. Hence

\displaystyle H_{q}(\sigma)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{p^{a}_{\sigma(i)}}{S_{\mathcal{U}}}\left((i-1)B_{q}+A_{q}\right).

Since
A_{q}
and
B_{q}/S_{\mathcal{U}}
do not depend on
\sigma
,
B_{q}>0
, and
p^{a}_{1}>p^{a}_{2}>\cdots>p^{a}_{k}
, we know that
H_{q}(\sigma)
is minimized only if
\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(r)
is the decreasing order of
p^{a}_{j}
on
{\mathcal{U}}
. So the unique optimal first-choice order at the current node is: among unresolved children, choose the one with largest
p^{a}_{j}
first, then the next largest, etc.

It remains to justify the recursive part. (i) If the chosen child
j
is correct and non-leaf, then after moving down we obtain a posterior in
{\mathcal{Q}}_{d-1,[k]}
, and by induction hypothesis, the unique optimal continuation from there is again the ranked DFS policy. (ii) If the chosen child
j
is incorrect, then after fully exploring it and returning, the posterior belongs to
{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}\setminus\{j\}}
, and by induction hypothesis on
(d,r-1)
, the unique optimal continuation is also the ranked DFS policy on the remaining unresolved children. This completes the induction.

Finally, we apply the claim at the root: for each fixed
l\in[L]
, let
q_{l}
denote the initial posterior law at the root
p_{\text{env,tree}}^{l}
. we have

\displaystyle\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)=H_{q_{l}}^{\pi}.

Since
q_{l}\in{\mathcal{Q}}_{l,[k]}
, the claim implies that
\pi^{\star}
uniquely minimizes
\overline{H}^{\pi}(l)
over
\Pi_{\text{success}}
. By (
731
) and our assumption that
\omega_{l}>0
for all
l\in[L]
we know that
\pi^{\star}
uniquely minimizes
H^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})
over
\Pi_{\text{success}}
.

Proof of part 2:
\pi^{\star}=\argmax_{\pi}J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})
for all
\gamma\in\left(1-\Theta\left(\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L}\right),1\right)
.

We show this by first showing for any
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}\setminus\{\pi^{\star}\}
,
J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})<J_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\star}}({\mathcal{P}})
, and then showing
\argmax_{\pi}J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\Pi_{\text{success}}
.

Step 1: show for any
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}\setminus\{\pi^{\star}\}
,
J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})<J_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\star}}({\mathcal{P}})
for all
\gamma\in(0,1)
.

Fix
\gamma\in(0,1)
. We use the same posterior family
{\mathcal{Q}}_{d,{\mathcal{U}}}
as in part 1, write
r\coloneqq|{\mathcal{U}}|
, and consider any deterministic order
\sigma=(\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(r))
in which a successful policy
\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}
inspects the children in
{\mathcal{U}}
before eventually going up. Let
W_{q}(i)
be the discounted return obtained if the correct child is inspected in position
i
, and after entering that child one continues recursively in the optimal way. By Fact 2 in part 1,
W_{q}(i)
depends only on the rank
i
, not on the label
\sigma(i)
. Moreover, since delaying the correct child by one extra wrong child inserts at least two extra steps (down and back up), and
\gamma\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle W_{q}(1)>W_{q}(2)>\cdots>W_{q}(r)>0.

(733)

Hence the discounted value of order
\sigma
is

\displaystyle V_{q}(\sigma)=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\frac{p^{a}_{\sigma(i)}}{S_{\mathcal{U}}}W_{q}(i).

By (
733
), (
26
) and rearrangement inequality we know that
V_{q}(\sigma)
is uniquely maximized by
\sigma(1),\cdots,\sigma(r)
given by decreasing order of
p^{a}_{j}
on
{\mathcal{U}}
. Then similar as the proof of part 1, applying this recursively after entering the correct child proves, by lexicographic induction on (
d
,
|{\mathcal{U}}|
), that

\displaystyle\pi^{\star}=\arg\max_{\pi\in\Pi_{\text{success}}}J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}}),\,\,\forall\gamma\in(0,1).

Step 2: show
\argmax_{\pi}J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\Pi_{\text{success}}
for all
\gamma\in\left(1-\Theta\left(\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L}\right),1\right)
.

We show this by fixing any policy
\pi\notin\Pi_{\text{success}}
and showing
\pi\notin\argmax_{\pi}J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})
. Since
\pi\notin\Pi_{\text{success}}
, it either (i) takes an illegal action at some (reachable, non-terminal) state at some node with positive probability, when there’s still untried actions at that node, or (ii) act legally when there’s still legal actions to take (with probability 1), but has positive probability to not explore all children of a node before going up at some state.

We let
J_{\gamma}^{\star}(s)
and
Q_{\gamma}^{\star}(s,a)
denote the optimal value and Q-function with discount factor
\gamma
at state
s
and state-action pair
(s,a)
respectively. We let
J^{\star}_{\gamma}({\mathcal{P}})
denote the optimal expected return with discount factor
\gamma
under the training distribution
{\mathcal{P}}
.
For case (i), consider any reachable non-terminal state
s
such that
\pi
assigns positive probability
p>0
to illegal actions at
s
. Let
h(s)
be the timestep of
s
, and let
d_{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi}(s)
be the probability that
s
is reached under
({\mathcal{P}},\pi)
. Define
\widetilde{\pi}
to agree with
\pi
before reaching
s
, and at
s
choose an action
a^{\star}\in\argmax_{a\in{\mathcal{A}}}Q_{\gamma}^{\star}(s,a)
, following an optimal continuation thereafter. Then, conditional on reaching
s
, policy
\pi
loses at least
pJ_{\gamma}^{\star}(s)
in continuation value compared to
\widetilde{\pi}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle J_{\gamma}^{\pi}({\mathcal{P}})\leq J_{\gamma}^{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathcal{P}})-d_{\mathcal{P}}^{\pi}(s)\gamma^{h(s)-1}pJ_{\gamma}^{\star}(s)<J_{\gamma}^{\widetilde{\pi}}({\mathcal{P}})\leq J_{\gamma}^{\star}({\mathcal{P}}).

Therefore, any policy that has positive probability to take illegal actions at a reachable, non-terminal state cannot be optimal.

Thus it remains to consider case (ii), i.e., the policies that act legally everywhere but are not successful. Without loss of generality, we assume at some reachable, non-terminal state
s
where the agent is at node
u
at level
h\in[l-1]
,
\pi
, for the first time, takes action
up
with probability 1 before exhausting all currently unresolved children at the current node
u
, Let
r
be the number of still-untried children at
u
, and let
d
be the remaining depth below
u
.

Construct a comparison policy
\pi^{\prime}
that is identical to
\pi
except at state
s
: instead of going up immediately, first explores the
r
remaining children under
u
in decreasing
p^{a}_{j}
-order, using
\pi^{\star}
recursively inside each such child subtree, and only after that returns to
u
and resumes
\pi
.
We let
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}
denote the event that the goal is in the untried children under
u
, and let
p_{s}\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}({\mathcal{E}}_{s}|s)
denote the probability that
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}
happens conditioned on the current state
s
. Let
R_{\gamma}(s)
denote the expected return of
\pi^{\prime}
conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}
. Let
R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s)
denote the expected return of
\pi
conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}^{c}
. Let
n_{s}
be the number of steps taken to finish exploring the remaining
r
children under
u
and return to
u
under event
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}
. Then the expected return of
\pi,\pi^{\prime}
at
s
, denoted by
G_{\gamma}^{\pi}(s)
and
G_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)
respectively, are

\displaystyle G_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)

\displaystyle=p_{s}R_{\gamma}(s)+(1-p_{s})\gamma^{n_{s}}R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s),\quad G_{\gamma}^{\pi}(s)=(1-p_{s})R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s).

(734)

We’ll show that when
\gamma\in\Big(1-\Theta\left(\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L}\right),1\Big)
,
G_{\gamma,l}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)>G_{\gamma,l}^{\pi}(s)
for all
l\in\{2,\cdots,L\}
. To do so, below we separately bounding
p_{s}
,
n_{s}
,
R_{\gamma}(s)
,
R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s)
.
We let
{\mathcal{U}}=\{n_{1},\cdots,n_{r}\}\subset[k]
denote the set of untried children of
u
with child indices
n_{1}<n_{2}<\cdots<n_{r}
, and write

\displaystyle P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})\coloneqq\sum_{n_{i}\in{\mathcal{U}}}p_{n_{i}}.

(735)

•

Bound
p_{s}
.

We define

\displaystyle q_{u}\coloneqq{\mathbb{P}}\left[\text{the goal is in the subtree rooted at }u|s\right].

Then we have

\displaystyle q_{u}\geq\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}.

(736)

Recall we assume it’s the first time for
\pi
to take action
up
before exploring all remaining children under
u
. (
735
) and (
736
) allow us to bound
p_{s}
as

\displaystyle p_{s}\geq\frac{q_{u}P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}{1-q_{u}+q_{u}P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}.

(737)

•

Bound
n_{s}
.
Let
h_{d}
be the (deterministic) number of steps needed to fully explore a perfect

k
-ary tree of depth
d

and return to the root (DFS tour length). A child subtree is explored by: go
\texttt{down}_{i}
(1 step) for all
i\in[k]
, explore that subtree and return (
h_{l}-1
steps), then go
up
(1 step), hence

\displaystyle h_{0}=0,\quad h_{d}=k(2+h_{d-1})=2k\frac{k^{d}-1}{k-1}.

(738)

Each wrong child subtree under
u
has remaining depth at most
d-1
. The largest possible cost to fully explore one such child subtree and return to
u
is attained by a perfect depth-(
d-1
) subtree, and equals
2+h_{d-1}
. Hence

\displaystyle n_{s}\leq r(2+h_{d-1}).

(739)

•

Bound
R_{\gamma}(s)
.

Let
\tau^{\star}_{d}
be the random hitting time of the ranked DFS policy
\pi^{\star}
on a perfect
k
-ary subtree of depth
d
conditioned on the event that the goal is in that subtree, with the clock started at the subtree root. It is easy to show by induction on
d
that among all full
k
-ary subtrees of remaining depth at most
d
, the perfect depth-
d
subtree gives the smallest discounted continuation value under
\pi^{\star}
: let
\tau^{\star}({\mathcal{T}})
denote the conditional hitting time on such a subtree
{\mathcal{T}}
, then
{\mathbb{E}}[\gamma^{\tau^{\star}({\mathcal{T}})}]\geq{\mathbb{E}}[\gamma^{\tau^{\star}_{d}}]
.
Then conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}
, recall we let
\pi^{\prime}
act the same as
\pi^{\star}
in every untried subtree under
u
, we have

\displaystyle R_{\gamma}(s)\geq\frac{1}{P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}\sum_{i=1}^{r}p_{n_{i}}^{a}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\gamma^{(2+h_{d-1})(i-1)+\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}\right]\geq\frac{p^{a}_{k}}{P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}\frac{1-\gamma^{(2+h_{d-1})r}}{1-\gamma^{2+h_{d-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\gamma^{\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}\right].

(740)

•

Bound
R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s)
.
Conditioned on
{\mathcal{E}}_{s}^{c}
, if
\pi
goes up immediately from
u
, then any eventual success can occur only after at least two more steps: one step up to the parent, and at least one step down into some other child subtree, because the goal is always a leaf. Therefore,

\displaystyle R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s)\leq\gamma^{2}.

(741)

Combine the bounds.

From (
739
), (
740
), (
741
) we deduce

\displaystyle G_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)-G_{\gamma}^{\pi}(s)

\displaystyle\overset{\eqref{eq:G_imbalanced}}{=}p_{s}R_{\gamma}(s)-(1-p_{s})\left(1-\gamma^{n_{s}}\right)R^{\prime}_{\gamma}(s)

\displaystyle\geq p_{s}\frac{p^{a}_{k}}{P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}\frac{1-\gamma^{(2+h_{d-1})r}}{1-\gamma^{2+h_{d-1}}}{\mathbb{E}}\left[\gamma^{\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}\right]-(1-p_{s})\left(1-\gamma^{(2+h_{d-1})r}\right)\gamma^{2}.

A sufficient condition for
G_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)>G_{\gamma}^{\pi}(s)
is

\displaystyle\frac{p_{s}}{1-p_{s}}>\frac{P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}{p^{a}_{k}}\frac{(1-\gamma^{(2+h_{d-1})})\gamma^{2}}{{\mathbb{E}}[\gamma^{\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}]}.

From (
737
) we know that

\displaystyle\frac{p_{s}}{1-p_{s}}\geq\frac{q_{u}P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}{1-q_{u}}\overset{\eqref{eq:q_u_imbalanced}}{\geq}\frac{\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}P_{r}({\mathcal{U}})}{1-\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}}.

Combining the above two relations, we know that it’s enough to require

\displaystyle\frac{\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}}{1-\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}}>\frac{1}{p^{a}_{k}}\frac{(1-\gamma^{(2+h_{d-1})})\gamma^{2}}{{\mathbb{E}}[\gamma^{\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}]}.

(742)

Now we bound
{\mathbb{E}}[\gamma^{\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}]
.
Define
T_{d}^{\star}\coloneqq{\mathbb{E}}[\tau_{d}^{\star}]
. Then we have

\displaystyle T_{0}^{\star}=0,\quad T^{\star}_{d}=T^{\star}_{d-1}+1+\sum_{j=1}^{k}p_{j}(j-1)(2+h_{d-1}),\,\,\forall d\geq 1.

This further yields

\displaystyle T_{d}^{\star}=d+\sum_{j=1}^{k}p_{j}(j-1)\sum_{i=0}^{d-1}(2+h_{i})\overset{\eqref{eq:h_d}}{=}d+\frac{2\sum_{j=1}^{k}p_{j}(j-1)}{k-1}\left(\frac{k(k^{d}-1)}{k-1}-d\right).

(743)

By Bernoulli’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\gamma^{h}\geq 1-h(1-\gamma),\,\,\forall h\geq 0,\quad\Longrightarrow\quad{\mathbb{E}}[\gamma^{\tau_{d-1}^{\star}}]\geq 1-T_{d-1}^{\star}(1-\gamma),

(744)

and

\displaystyle(1-\gamma^{2+h_{d-1}})\gamma^{2}\leq 1-\gamma^{2+h_{d-1}}\leq(2+h_{d-1})(1-\gamma).

(745)

Substituting (
744
) and (
745
) into (
742
), a sufficient condition for
G_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)>G_{\gamma}^{\pi}(s)
is

\displaystyle\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d+1}(1-T^{\star}_{d-1}(1-\gamma))>\left(1-\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}\right)(2+h_{d-1})(1-\gamma),

which is equivalent to

\displaystyle\gamma>1-\underbrace{\frac{\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d+1}}{\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d+1}T^{\star}_{d-1}+\left(1-\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d}\right)(2+h_{d-1})}}_{\coloneqq\delta_{d}},\,\,\forall d\in[L].

Define

\displaystyle\delta\coloneqq\min_{d\in[L]}\delta_{d}.

(746)

Then a sufficient condition for
G_{\gamma}^{\pi^{\prime}}(s)>G_{\gamma}^{\pi}(s)
is

\displaystyle\gamma>1-\delta.

(747)

Below we show

\displaystyle\delta=\Theta\left(\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L}\right).

(748)

When
d=1
, since
h_{0}=T_{0}^{\star}=0
, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{1}=\frac{\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L}}{2\left(1-\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-1}\right)}=\Theta\left(\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L}\right).

(749)

For
d\geq 2
, by (
738
) and (
743
) we know that

\displaystyle h_{d-1}=\Theta\left(k^{d-1}\right),\,\,\,T_{d-1}^{\star}=O\left(k^{d-1}\right),

so

\displaystyle\delta_{d}=\Theta\left(\frac{\left(p^{a}_{k}\right)^{L-d+1}}{k^{d-1}}\right),\,\,\forall 2\leq d\leq L.

(750)

From (
746
), (
749
), (
750
) and the fact that
p_{k}<1/k
we know that (
748
) holds.

Appendix C
Proof of Construction

Notation.

We’ll reuse some notations defined at the beginning of Appendix
A.2
, which we copy below for reader’s convenience. We define

\pi_{h}\coloneqq\pi_{\theta}(E^{(h)}),\,\forall h\in[H].
and
N_{h}
as the number of leaf nodes explored up to step
h
:

\displaystyle N_{h,\times}\coloneqq\sum_{i=1}^{h}\mathbbm{1}\{c_{i}=\times\}.

(751)

At any step
h
, the agent is at node
n_{h}
, and we let

\displaystyle{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\coloneqq\begin{cases}\{i\in[k]|i\text{ is taken at }n_{h}\},\quad\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
\emptyset,\quad\text{if }c_{h}=\times,\\
\end{cases}

(752)

denote the set of downward actions that are taken at node
n_{h}
. Define

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k+1]:\quad N_{h}(i)\coloneqq\sum_{j=1}^{h-1}\mathbbm{1}\{a_{j}=i\}

(753)

as the number of times action
i
is taken up to step
h
. Let
N_{h}(\texttt{up})\coloneqq N_{h}(k+1)
.

We’ll make use of the following lemma from
23
to show the existence of our matrix constructions.

Lemma 9

(Restatement of Lemma 1 in
23
)
.

Suppose matrices
X\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}\times n_{1}},Y\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{2}\times n_{2}}
have full column rank. Then for any matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{1}\times n_{2}}
, there exists a matrix
M\in\mathbb{R}^{m_{1}\times m_{2}}
such that

X^{\top}MY=A.

C.1
Proof of Theorem
1

First of all, from Lemma
9
, the definition of
A_{B},A_{C},A_{P},A_{Q}
in (
2.3
) and the full column rank assumption on
U,V,Z
, we know that our construction (
1
) exists. We use
O(f(k))
to denote a quantity that is bounded above by a constant multiple of
f(k)
for any
k\geq 2
(we don’t require
k
to be large).

Fix any full
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
of depth
l\in[L]
and any goal leaf
g({\mathcal{T}})
. Consider any legal trajectory generated by policy
\pi
on
{\mathcal{T}}
.
From the proof of Lemma
1
we know that the policy expressions in Lemma
1
still hold here with
a_{b,2}=a_{p,\texttt{up}}=a_{p,0}=a_{p,2}=0
, i.e., we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{h}}\exp\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\xi_{h}\right),

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k]:\quad\pi_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{h}}\exp\left(\frac{-\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}a_{p,1}(e^{a_{b,1}}-1)-N_{h}(i)a_{p,1}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right),

(754)

where

\displaystyle\xi_{h}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}a_{q,\times}-(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}a_{q,0}}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}}a_{q,\times}-(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}}a_{q,0}}{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times,\end{cases}

(755)

and
Z_{h}
is the normalizing factor:

\displaystyle Z_{h}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\exp\left(\left(1+\frac{1}{k}\right)\xi_{h}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{\varphi_{h}(i)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right).

(756)

Note that the trajectory length
H
is upper bounded by
2N
, and
N_{h,\times}\leq\lfloor\frac{h}{2}\rfloor
, as under our setting,
c_{1}=0
, and two wrong-leaf observations cannot occur in consecutive timesteps.
Therefore, by our choice of
a_{q,0},a_{q,\times},a_{c,0}
in (
1
) and (
755
), we have

\displaystyle\xi_{h}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0},&\text{if }c_{h}=0,\\
\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,\times},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times.\end{cases}

(757)

For each step
h\in[H]
, if the trajectory has not terminated yet, only four cases would occur: (i)
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
: the agent enters a non-leaf node for the first time; (ii)
c_{h}=\times,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
: the agent is at a non-goal leaf; (iii)
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
: the agent is at a non-leaf node and has already explored part of its children; (iv)
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k
: the agent is at a non-leaf node and has explored all
k
children.

For case (i), from (
C.1
), (
757
) we know that when
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
,

\displaystyle\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{-N_{h}(i)a_{p,1}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+h-1}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)+\exp\left(\frac{-\min_{i\in[k]}\{N_{h}(i)\}a_{p,1}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+h-1}\right)}.

(758)

Note that

\displaystyle\min_{i\in[k]}\{N_{h}(i)\}\leq\frac{h-1-N_{h}(\texttt{up})}{k}.

(759)

Thus by our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{q,0},a_{b,0}
in (
1
) and (
C.1
), we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(760)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
1
). This suggests under our construction, when the agents enter a new non-leaf node, it will explore its children instead of going up with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

Similarly, for case (ii), all actions other than
up
are illegal, and from (
C.1
), (
757
) and our choice of
a_{q,\times},a_{p,1}
in (
1
) we know that when
c_{h}=\times
,

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{-N_{h}(i)a_{p,1}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+h-1}\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,\times}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{-N_{h}(i)a_{p,1}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+h-1}\right)}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(761)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
1
). This suggests the agent will go up at any non-goal leaf with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

For case (iii),
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
, from (
C.1
) and our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{b,0},a_{b,1}
in (
1
) we know that when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
,

\displaystyle\forall i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}:\quad\pi_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{h}}\exp\left(\frac{-a_{p,1}\left(e^{a_{b,1}}-1+N_{h}(i)\right)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(\frac{-a_{p,1}\left(e^{a_{b,1}}-1+N_{h}(i)\right)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right)}{\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}\exp\left(\frac{-a_{p,1}N_{h}(j)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)a_{p,1}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\exp\left(-\frac{1}{k-1}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)a_{p,1}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2kN}

(762)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
1
). Analogously, we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}{\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}\exp\left(\frac{-a_{p,1}N_{h}(j)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|}\right)}

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2kN},

(763)

where the second line uses our choice of
a_{q,0},a_{p,1},a_{b,0},a_{b,1}
in (
1
) as well as the fact that

\displaystyle N_{h}(j)\leq\frac{N}{k},\,\,\forall j\in[k].

(764)

(
C.1
) and (
C.1
) together indicate in case (iii), the agent will choose to visit the unexplored children with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

For case (iv), from (
C.1
) and our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{b,0},a_{b,1}
in (
1
) we deduce when
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k
,

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{-a_{p,1}\left(e^{a_{b,1}}-1+N_{h}(i)\right)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+ke^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-k}\right)}{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\frac{-a_{p,1}\left(e^{a_{b,1}}-1+N_{h}(i)\right)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+ke^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-k}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{k\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)\frac{a_{p,1}}{k}\right)}{\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(765)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
1
). This suggests the agent will go up at any non-leaf node after fully exploring its children with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

Note that the trajectory length can be bounded by
H=2N
. From the above discussion on the four cases, we know that given any
\Phi=({\mathcal{T}},g({\mathcal{T}}))
where
{\mathcal{T}}
is a full
k
-ary tree with depth
l\in[L]
, the agent will perform DFS on
{\mathcal{T}}
with probability at least
1-\epsilon
until the goal is found. This completes the proof of Theorem
1
.

C.2
Proof of Theorem
4

First, from Lemma
9
, the definition of
A_{B},A_{C},A_{P},A_{Q}
in (
2.3
) and the full column rank assumption on
U,V,Z
, we know that our construction (
4
) exists.

Fix any full
k
-ary tree
{\mathcal{T}}
of depth
l\in[L]
and any goal leaf
g({\mathcal{T}})
. Consider any legal trajectory generated by policy
\pi
on
{\mathcal{T}}
. To better align with the node/action/label numbering, we let
A_{P},A_{Q}
’s column indices be 0-indexed, and
A_{P},A_{Q}
’s row indices be 1-indexed, and we let
\times
and
\checkmark
denote index 1 and 2 respectively, e.g.,
A_{Q}(:,0)
and
A_{Q}(:,\times)
denote the first and second columns of
A_{Q}
respectively. We let
up
denote the action index
k+1
.

By (
11
), (
2.3
) and (
4
) we have

\displaystyle\pi_{h}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{softmax}\left(\mu_{h}+\nu_{h}\right),

(766)

where

\displaystyle\mu_{h}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\frac{A_{P}(:,0)e^{a_{b,0}}+\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}A_{P}(:,j)e^{a_{b,1}}+\sum_{i=1}^{k}\left(N_{h}(i)-\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}\right)A_{P}(:,i)}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|},

(767)

and

\displaystyle\nu_{h}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\begin{cases}\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}A_{Q}(:,\times)+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}A_{Q}(:,0)}{N_{h,\times}e^{-a_{c,0}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{a_{c,0}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=0\\
\frac{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}}A_{Q}(:,\times)+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}}A_{Q}(:,0)}{N_{h,\times}e^{a_{c,1}}+(h-N_{h,\times})e^{-a_{c,1}}},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times\end{cases}.

(768)

Combining (
4
) with the above two expressions, we can further simplify
\mu_{h}
and
\nu_{h}
as

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k]:\quad\mu_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=\frac{-\mathbbm{1}\{i\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\}a_{p,1}(e^{a_{b,1}}-1)-N_{h}(i)a_{p,1}}{e^{a_{b,0}}+|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|e^{a_{b,1}}+h-1-|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|},\quad\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})=0,

(769)

and

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k]:\quad\nu_{h}(i)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)\lambda_{i},&\text{if }c_{h}=0\\
O\left(\frac{1}{k\ln^{2}k}\right)\lambda_{i},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times\end{cases},

\displaystyle\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0},&\text{if }c_{h}=0\\
\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{k\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,\times},&\text{if }c_{h}=\times\end{cases}.

(770)

Same as in the proof of Theorem
1
in Appendix
C.1
, for each step
h\in[H]
, if the trajectory has not terminated yet, only four cases would occur: (i)
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
: the agent enters a non-leaf node for the first time; (ii)
c_{h}=\times,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
: the agent is at a non-goal leaf; (iii)
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
: the agent is at a non-leaf node and has already explored part of its children; (iv)
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k
: the agent is at a non-leaf node and has explored all
k
children.

For case (i), from (
766
), (
769
), (
C.2
), our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{b,0},a_{q,0},\lambda_{i}
in (
4
) and the fact that

N_{h}(i)\leq N/k\,\,\forall i\in[k]

we know that when
c_{h}=0,|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=0
,

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(1)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})+\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})\right)+\sum_{i=2}^{k}\exp\left(\mu_{h}(i)+\nu_{h}(i)\right)}{\exp\left(\mu_{h}(1)+\nu_{h}(1)\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(\exp\left(-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}\right)+\sum_{i=2}^{k}\exp\left(\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)\lambda_{i}\right)\right)}{\exp\left(\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)\lambda_{1}\right)}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(771)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
4
). This suggests when entering a non-leaf node for the first time, the agent will act the same as the optimal policy
\pi^{\star}
and explore its first child with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

Similarly, For case (ii), from (
766
),(
769
) (
C.2
), our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{b,0},\lambda_{i},a_{q,\times}
in (
4
) we know that when
c_{h}=\times
,

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\mu_{h}(i)+\nu_{h}(i)\right)}{\exp\left(\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})+\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})\right)}\leq\frac{k}{\exp\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)a_{q,\times}\right)}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(772)

given constant
C
large enough in (
4
). This suggests when at a non-goal leaf, the agent will act the same as the optimal policy
\pi^{\star}
and go up with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

For case (iii) where the agent is at a node whose
i\coloneqq|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|\in[k-1]
children have been explored, we let
m
be the smallest index among legal action set
[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}
. Then from (
766
) we deduce

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\exp\left(\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})+\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})\right)+\sum_{j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}\exp\left(\mu_{h}(j)+\nu_{h}(j)\right)+\sum_{j\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}\cup\{m\}}\exp\left(\mu_{h}(j)+\nu_{h}(j)\right)}{\exp\left(\mu_{h}(m)+\nu_{h}(m)\right)}.

(773)

From (
769
), (
C.2
), our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{b,0},a_{b,1},\lambda_{i},a_{q,0}
in (
4
) we know that

\displaystyle\forall j\in{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}:\quad\mu_{h}(j)+\nu_{h}(j)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{i}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)a_{p,1}+\lambda_{j},

\displaystyle\forall j\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}:\quad\mu_{h}(j)+\nu_{h}(j)

\displaystyle=O\left(\frac{1}{k\ln^{2}k}\right)a_{p,1}+\lambda_{j},

\displaystyle\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})+\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle=-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}.

(774)

Substituting (
C.2
) into (
C.2
), and by our choice of index
m
and
a_{p,1},a_{q,0},\lambda_{i}
in (
4
) we get

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(m)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(775)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
4
). This implies when at a node whose children are only partially visited, the agent will choose the child
m
with the smallest index among the unexplored children (i.e.,
m=\argmax_{j\in[k]\setminus{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}}p^{a}_{j}
), same as what
\pi^{\star}
does, with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

For case (iv) where the agent is at a node whose
k
children all have been explored (
|{\mathcal{A}}_{h,\times}|=k
), from (
769
), (
C.2
), (
4
) we know that

\displaystyle\forall i\in[k]:\quad\mu_{h}(i)+\nu_{h}(i)=-\frac{1}{k}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln k}\right)\right)a_{p,1}+\lambda_{i},

\displaystyle\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})+\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})=-\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{\ln^{2}k}\right)\right)a_{q,0}.

(776)

By the above relation, (
766
) and our choice of
a_{p,1},a_{q,0},\lambda_{i}
in (
4
) we have

\displaystyle 1-\pi_{h}(\texttt{up})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sum_{i=1}^{k}\exp\left(\mu_{h}(i)+\nu_{h}(i)\right)}{\exp\left(\mu_{h}(\texttt{up})+\nu_{h}(\texttt{up})\right)}\leq\frac{\epsilon}{2N}

(777)

when constant
C
is large enough in (
4
). This implies when at a node whose children all have been explored, the agent will again act the same as
\pi^{\star}
and go up with probability at least
1-\frac{\epsilon}{2N}
.

Putting the results of the above four cases together, and using the fact that the trajectory length is upper bounded by
H=2N
, we know that (
32
) in Theorem
4
holds under our construction.
